戰火影響料屬短期，無礙市場中期走勢
2026 年 4 月
美國對伊朗展開襲擊所引發的中東戰火延續至今，拖累 3 月截至 27 日的全球金融股市表現，
市場在多重變數交織下動能顯著受壓，主要股市指數單月報酬全盤皆墨。不過，在能源類股大漲的支
撐下，S&P 500 指數表現相對其他股市穩定。日本股市雖亦出現獲利了結壓力，但受惠於高市政府
「負責且積極的財政政策」 及日圓匯率弱勢有利出口 ， 今年以來 MSCI 日本指數的表現僅次於拉丁美
洲股市。另一方面，受油價高漲帶動通膨預期、外資持續撤出且經濟放緩所累，印度股市出現賣壓，

MSCI 印度指數不論在 3 月或是今年至今表現都是敬陪末座。
全球主要債市 3 月報酬亦全數下跌。在所有券種之中 ， 以新興當地債下跌 5.21% 表現最為疲弱 ，
除了油價引發市場對於進口原油的新興國家前景的擔憂外 ，高漲的美元指數也使得當地貨幣計價新興
債表現呈壓。 全球主權債同樣因為油價高漲推升通膨疑慮 ，投資人對於各國央行貨幣政策或因而轉為
更為鷹派高度警戒，使其表現差強人意。
自去年川普關稅「解放日」後美股跌至低點後開始走揚，各國股市與主要指數持續上揚，其中又
以金礦指數、南韓股市、費半指數、日本股市表現最佳。然而，自中東戰事 2/28 爆發後，以指數修
正幅度來看，跌幅最大的市場分別為金礦指數、南韓股市、日本股市。唯有費半指數則在 AI 需求暢
旺的加持下成為例外。由此可見，本波市場的回跌，基本上為「漲多跌多」的修正效應。
就基本面來看，2026 年美股企業獲利增長主軸仍在 AI， 由於2026 年仍在 AI 基礎建設期擴張間 ，
AI 終端需求仍是科技龍頭企業，故所帶動的獲利增長料不致受油價影響，今年單季盈餘增長可望維

持 2 位數水準。自中東戰事以來，彭博對於 MSCI 美國指數 Q1 及 Q2 的 EPS 預期不跌反漲，顯示市
場對於美國企業的獲利前景仍極具信心。同時，目前的資訊科技類股的預期 P/E 比與過去 10 年的均
值相較仍過有遭到低估之嫌，也預示科技類股相較於其他類股仍有進一步向上的動能。
再者，儘管近期美國公債殖利率因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目
標，投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策
預期變動影響較大，故在年期與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

美國股市：標普 500 企業盈餘增長加速，私募信貸贖回潮不致衝擊美國經濟
美國第四季 GDP 季增年率修正值 0.7%，較初值 1.4%有所調降，主要反映實質個人消費季比降
至 0.7%。第四季 GDP 數據整體仍支持美國經濟穩健。但第一季受通膨(關稅與伊朗危機)與高利率影
響，消費行為呈現「降級消費」特徵，非必需性支出收縮，經濟呈現倚賴「高收入族群支撐消費」的
K 型分化格局。由於中東戰火推高能源價格，美國民眾擔心生活成本壓力惡化，美國密西根大學3 月
消費者信心指數降至 55.5，較 2 月終值 56.6 下降 1.1，觸及今年以來最低紀錄，反映中東危機與生

活成本壓力削弱民眾樂觀情緒。
2 月非農就業人數淨減少 9.2 萬個，低於彭博預期的增加 1.3 萬，失業率躍升至 4.4%，失業率雖
短期上揚、但就長期角度仍在低檔。追究原因數據主要是受到罷工和天氣等短期因素干擾，導致醫療
健保、建築業、零售業和休閒酒店等產業表現不佳，但整體勞動市場狀態溫和，從請領就業金人數等
數據看來就業市場尚未出現顯著惡化跡象。市場先前擔憂 AI 取代人力，目前數據看來企業僅是招募
保守，後續可觀察勞動市場降溫是否反映在薪資成長，若薪資壓力下降有助於抑制服務業通膨，除直
接改善企業成本結構外，也將使聯準會擔憂的服務業通膨出現放緩。
預估第一季標普 500 企業餘年增仍可達到 11.3%，且在第二季顯著拉升至近兩成水準，科技板
塊受惠於 AI 仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸。大型科技巨擘透過AI 優化流程，開始對利潤率產生正
面影響。然而，此一效益目前高度集中於資本與技術優勢明顯的科技龍頭企業，中小企業與非科技產
業尚未顯著受益。因此，企業獲利將呈現明顯分化格局，而尚未受惠於 AI 的非科技業者，將會在未

來幾年逐步提高使用 AI 程度，傳統企業大規模導入 AI 模型與使用算力，將可成為 AI 龍頭下一波業
績成長動能。
近期美國私募信貸市場爆發贖回潮，包含貝萊德、阿波羅、藍鷹(Blue Owl)等巨頭均遭投資人要
求提款，甚至限制贖回以防止流動性枯竭。此波動盪主因投資人擔憂 AI 技術衝擊軟體業導致違約風
險上升，以及對高收益資產的獲利了結。研判此類事件雖造成金融股拉回，但引發總體系統性危機的
可能性極低。
目前標普 500 本益比估計在 22.84 倍、股價淨值比 4.95 倍，短期評價偏高，面對伊朗戰事的干
擾，預料股市震盪加劇，但若市場拉回，基於美國在 AI 產業具備絕對優勢，仍建議以長線角度布局。

歐洲股市：歐元區經濟表現穩健，央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度
歐元區第四季 GDP 季增率自初值的 0.3%下修至 0.2%，年增則下修至 1.2%，二者皆低於市場預
期。歐元區第四季經濟動能略微放緩，成長主要由內需帶動。家庭消費在穩健的勞動力市場與薪資增
長支撐下，季增率上升至 0.2%，固定資本形成雖由高點回落，但仍維持正成長，外部需求疲軟導致
出口季減，加上企業去庫存，顯示內需支撐正與貿易逆風強烈拉鋸。
三月份歐元區經濟活動連續 15 個月處於擴張區間，其中製造業表現強勁，歐元區製造業PMI 由
50.8 上升至 51.4，創 45 個月高點，受新訂單增長支撐，惟出口訂單連續第 49 個月低於榮枯線，外

需疲軟仍未改善。服務業受中東戰爭引發的不確定性與能源成本飆升衝擊，驟降至 50.1，放緩至 10
個月低點。預料南歐以觀光業為主國家將面對能源成本衝擊，而外銷為主的如德國、法國將迎來能源
與運輸成本飆升的雙重打擊。歐盟決定延後提交原定 4/15 提出將俄羅斯石油進口永久禁令入法的提
案，自 2022 年俄羅斯全面入侵烏克蘭以來，歐盟已大幅降低對俄能源依賴，是否因為伊朗戰爭調整
制裁內容將是未來關注重點。
歐洲央行在 3 月利率決策會議決定利率不變、但態度轉鷹。歐洲央行總裁拉加德表示，若通膨出
現「顯著但不過於持久」的超標情況，可能進行「適度的政策調整」，以確保通膨預期維持穩定。她
強調 ， 若對通膨偏離目標完全不作反應 ， 恐影響政策溝通與市場信心 ， 使外界難以理解央行反應機制 。
從利率期貨觀察 ， 市場預期歐洲央行與英國央行都有機會在第二季升息1 碼 ， 不排除未來一年升息到
2 碼，顯示歐洲央行與英國央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度。
在地緣風險常態化背景下，歐洲各國持續提高國防支出，德國政府規劃在2026 年投入 380 億歐

元於基礎建設、氣候保護專項基金、國防及數位化，加上 AI 浪潮帶動企業投資，投資者原本對歐洲
經濟抱持樂觀態度，然而油價大漲大幅扭轉市場樂觀預期。 考量市場目前悲觀原因仍是地緣政治風
險，假設美伊衝突能在第二季落幕，對歐股企業獲利影響有限，歐洲經濟將在下半年重回成長軌道。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.14，故價淨值比為 2.27。就評價角度相對合理，雖然年初以來歐
股的樂觀情緒因為伊朗戰爭快速扭轉，但未來若油價回落，或是歐洲重新考慮向俄羅斯購買能源，都
能夠化解歐洲面臨的能源困境，故仍可視為長線佈局標的。

日本股市：高市政府加大戰略產業投資，政府補貼壓抑油價衝擊
日本受惠於企業強勁支出 ，2025 年第四季 GDP 年化季增率為 1.3%， 優於原先預估的0.2%。2026
年不僅企業資本支出動能持續 ， 在高市早苗出任首相後 ， 政府選定支持包含實體人工智慧 、 再生醫學 、
量子運算與海洋無人機等 61 個項目，目標在 2040 年前取得全球 AI 機器人逾 30%的市占率，國產
晶片銷售額也要在同年衝上 40 兆日圓。高市希望藉由政府與民間的合作推動投資，強化發展包含人
工智慧、半導體、宇宙及航太等 17 大重點產業。由於自民黨已在國會選舉取得空前壓倒性勝利，產

業補貼計畫將可落實，讓政府投資不僅貢獻短期 GDP，亦可提升日本長期經濟競爭力。
日本 2 月剔除生鮮食品的消費者價格年比上漲 1.6%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最小升幅，主要因為
公用事業補貼壓低能源成本。日本勞動組合總連合會(連合)近期公布 2026 年春鬥第 1 次彙整結果，
加薪幅度雖低於去年同期 、 不過已連3 年高於 5%，且2026 年中小企業加薪幅度可望高於過去幾年，
支撐日本薪資物價形成正向循環，並達成實質薪資增長。近期伊朗戰事導致油價飆升，首相高市早苗
積極應對，除釋出戰備儲油外，將控制汽油價格上限，故預料短期通膨不致大幅躍昇。
日本央行三月利率決策會議維持政策不變，主要考量來自中東局勢不確定性，難以衡量伊朗戰爭
及國際油價飆漲對日本經濟及通膨帶來影響 ，呼應日本央行總裁植田和男論點 ， 希望有更多時間可以
觀察對物價的引導。後市央行升息時點須留意4 月初將公布的短觀報告，若企業信心尚維持正面，搭
配伊朗戰爭如白宮所預期的在 5、6 周之內結束，則日本央行或有望在第二季升息一碼。預料升息進

度放緩將使十年期日本公債殖利率在 2.0~2.5%區間震盪，且由於全球央行方向轉鷹、美元受惠於避
險需求，日本央行緩步升息有助日圓匯率穩定。
波克夏將對東京海上進行出資 ， 在4 月藉由收購東京海上持有的自家庫藏股 、 取得東京海上2.49%
股權，出資額為 2,874 億日圓。在巴菲特掌舵時期，波克夏對日本企業的投資以大型商社為主，此次
將是波克夏首度和日本金融機構進行合作 ， 性質與過去投資商社股不同 ， 若波克夏未來對日投資可擴
及其他非商社股，對於日股長期表現將帶來更大力度的加持。
2026 年以來日股受惠於高市擴大財政刺激 、強化軍事安全、鞏固美日同盟上，但本益比仍在 18.38
倍，股價淨值比在 1.68 倍。3 月下旬外資賣超日股，估計是受到伊朗戰事干擾且避免股利發放的課
稅而調整持股部位所致。近期日圓震盪走貶，有利於於日本外銷股財報表現，預料企業獲利可望為日
股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

新興亞洲股市：中東戰事對經濟影響負面，但成長仍具韌性
亞洲股市 3 月因中東情勢緊張而大跌，截至 3/27，亞洲不含日本指數 (MXAPJ) 單月下跌
10.39%。南韓股市跌幅居前，單月下跌 12.90%，多次於盤中啟動熔斷機制，除了中東地緣政治風
險之外，漲幅過大後的技術性修正也是主因。台股在內資支撐下，跌幅相對較輕，單月下跌 6.50%。
中國股市也表現弱勢，上證指數下跌 5.99%，國企指數下跌 4.58%。3 月份正值全國兩會結束，股市
出現了「利多出盡」的調節。MSCI 東協指數 3 月下跌 3.53%，是跌幅較輕的亞洲區域。馬來西亞與

印尼身為原油、液化天然氣（LNG）及棕櫚油的重要出口國，油價上漲直接帶動其相關權值股走強，
抵銷了跌幅，但越南、菲律賓等原油進口國，跌幅超過 10%。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌 9.48%，印度
是全球第三大石油進口國，約 85% 的原油依賴進口，油價高漲對經濟造衝擊，另外外資於三月大量
賣出，也是印度股市重挫的原因。
亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 將面對能源價格的劇烈波動對經濟的衝擊。根據 ADB 資料，泰國、
南韓與新加坡及印度等國，其能源進口依賴度顯著高於區域平均 。在當前中東衝突陰影未散的背景
下，油價的每一次跳動都觸發亞洲通膨的扳機，對於這些原油高依賴進口國家而言，能源成本的上升
不僅僅是物價問題，更是對 GDP 增長動能的直接侵蝕。
根據野村的推估「油價每上漲 10%」對各國經濟的衝擊路徑各不相同，印度與菲律賓的 CPI 通
膨壓力最為顯著，預計分別跳升 0.5 個百分點，這將迫使兩國央行在 Q2 維持較高利率環境，壓縮
降息空間 。反觀馬來西亞與印尼，受惠於其資源生產國的地位，GDP 增長甚至可能出現微幅的正向

回饋或較小的負面衝擊 。這種「能源非對稱性」將導致亞洲內部經濟與貨幣政策的進一步分化。
然而亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 面臨增長放緩的挑戰。但根據博鰲亞洲論壇報告表示，受國際貿易不
確定性及地緣政治風險上升影響，今年亞洲增長率預計從去年的 4.7% 降至 4.5%。儘管增速微降，
但亞洲經濟體 GDP 占世界比重持續上升，依然是全球經濟的主要成長引擎。這與 ADB 資料相呼
應，顯示亞洲在全球經濟增速放緩的大環境下，憑藉區域一體化進程展現出較佳的韌性 。
對於印度與菲律賓等高通膨敏感區，應關注其內需韌性能否抵銷能源稅的負面影響；而對於新加
坡、南韓與台灣等雖然進口佔比較高但產業鏈地位穩固的經濟體，仍看好在全球 AI 供應鏈帶來的快
速增長。整體而言亞洲國家在 2026 年的經濟增長整體保持韌性，但將出現明確分化。

中國股市：轉型陣痛與外部地緣風險的夾擊
2026 年中國兩會不僅發佈經濟目標，更是「十五五規劃」首年的轉型宣言。官方將 GDP 成長
目標設定在 4.5% 至 5.0% 的區間，這項數據釋放了一個明確信號，北京已不再盲目追求過往的高
速增長，而是將重心置於「新質生產力」的質變與落地。在政策宣示中，我們觀察到由「總量擴張」
向 「結構優化」 的顯著移轉。2026 年數位經濟核心產業佔 GDP 比重目標提升至 12.5%，重點布局
人工智能（AI Agent）與具身智能（Physical AI）的工業化應用。同時，政府推動的大規模「設備更

新與消費品以舊換新」 政策，試圖在傳統房產動能消退之際，透過激發存量市場的替代需求來維持內
需熱度。這種以新經濟與高品質消費為主的政策組合，旨在對沖房地產下行對內需產生的結構性缺
口，並賦予國家經濟轉型的重任。
中國經濟正經歷一場歷史性的動能切換，長期作為增長引擎的房地產，其最終需求對 GDP 的佔
比正持續回落。與此同時，以醫藥、先進設備、IT 通訊及研發為首的「高科技 + 新三樣」產業，其
貢獻權重已經在 2025 年正式超越房地產。轉型過程雖然伴隨著陣痛，卻是中國邁向科技自主的必要
路徑 。 儘管房地產的長尾效應仍在消化 ， 但這些新興領域的快速擴張正為中國經濟建立更具韌性的基
礎，成為觀察中國能否維持區域增速預期的核心支柱。對於投資者而言，這意味著舊有的投資邏輯已
徹底改變，未來市場的超額收益將高度集中在具備全球競爭力的先進製造龍頭。
儘管新經濟轉型積極，但房地產市場的下滑壓力依然是中國最沉重的負擔。根據彭博模型，房地
產尋底之路極為漫長。預測路徑顯示，未來幾年房地產佔 GDP 比重將持續收縮至 12% 左右。中

國的政策博弈重點在於政府如何透過擴張性財政手段來進行 「止血」 ，防止房市超跌引發系統性金融
風險。但在目前市場信心修復極為緩慢的背景下，觀察銷售數據能否在超跌區間出現初步止穩。
然而中國面臨的外部環境更加嚴峻。中東危機的突然升溫，成為干擾復甦的關鍵變數。地緣震盪
推升了能源溢價，加劇輸入型通膨壓力，美國聯準會（Fed）態度的轉向鷹派與美元持續走強，顯著
壓縮了中國央行的降息空間。在強美元與高利差的雙重擠壓下，人民幣匯率面臨巨大的貶值壓力。這
種「內需待援、外部受限」的政策困局，使得操作空間顯得倍加受限，雖然兩會釋放了擴張性財政的
積極信號，但在地緣政治風險與強勢美元背景下，市場缺乏強勁的資金流入動能，也因此我們認為中
國股市欠缺全面性上漲的機會。類股建議在配置上規避傳統房地產產業，鎖定受惠於新質生產力政
策、具備技術護城河且能抵禦外部波動的科技領先標的。

印度股市： 中東戰事與 Fed 轉鷹影響印度成長動能
印度的實體經濟活動成長發出趨緩信號。3 月份最新發布的 HSBC Flash 印度綜合 PMI 指數由 2
月的 58.9 降至 56.5，創下 2022 年 10 月以來最弱的擴張速度，反映出國內需求在物價壓力下正
逐步降溫 。其中，製造業 PMI 降至 53.8，顯示國內訂單成長受阻；然而，值得關注的是國際訂單
卻創下調查歷史新高，這證明在全球供應鏈重組的戰略大環境下，印度製造的外部需求依然堅挺 。
儘管基本面仍具韌性，但 PMI 所揭示的投入成本增速創多年新高，預示著企業利潤空間將面臨嚴峻

的輸入型通膨擠壓，這將是觀察印度經濟能否維持高成長的首要指標。
儘管短期數據出現波動，但印度作為全球增長引擎的核心地位並未動搖。在 2026-27 財政預算
案的架構下， 政府持續推動以基礎設施投資為核心的擴張性政策 ， 試圖透過提升公共支出效率來強化
製造業基礎。印度正積極利用其人口紅利與政策激勵，吸引從電子組裝到半導體測試的全球業者進
駐。然而，這種高度依賴資本流入與外部能源供給的增長模式，在地緣政治動盪與聯準會轉向鷹派的
環境下，正迎來一場壓力測試。「內部政策利多」與「外部環境利空」的互相干擾，使得印度的成長
前景短期面臨不確定性 。
能源價格震盪是印度經濟短期最重要的潛在風險。根據彭博經濟的報導，印度央行估計，原油價
格每上漲 10 美元，經濟成長將會下降 0.15%。若中東衝突導致霍爾木茲海峽關閉三個月，2027 財
年成長率恐重挫至 6.1% 。在極端情況下，燃料成本飆升將引發連鎖反應，不僅削弱消費者的實際收
入，更會因國營石油公司虧損削減股利，進而擠壓預算案中的公共建設支出與政府投資能力。除了價

格波動，供應端的物理性中斷亦是威脅。印度的能源約 85%依賴進口，若航運中斷導致供應缺口，
包含民生消費與石化產業等經濟活動都將受到供應鍊斷裂的風險。
在貨幣政策上 ， 印度央行面臨 「內外夾擊」 。 若油價衝向140 美元 ，CPI 預計將進一步升 5.0%，
並可能在 9 月突破 RBI 的 6%容忍區間，央行被迫在年底臨時升息 2 碼。外部方面，聯準會態度的轉
向鷹派加劇了資金回流美元資產的壓力，也將干擾印度央行的寬鬆節奏。綜合上述分析，我們對印度
市場看法轉向中性。雖然結構性紅利與財政擴張確保了其增長基本盤 ， 但近期的重心已由 「追求擴張」
轉向「防禦通膨與能源衝擊」，這讓 PMI 顯示內需趨緩。加上潛在的油價的大漲與供應減少，正大
幅抵銷供應鏈轉移的長期利多。我們建議在股市配置上採取守勢。在近期的估值修正期，利用市場震
盪，重新評估基本面。

拉美股市：巴西正式啟動降息循環，中東衝突有利拉美經濟
巴西央行貨幣政策委員會(Copom)於 3 月 18 日會議全票通過將基準利率調降 25 個基點至
14.75%，正式啟動新一輪降息循環。Copom 會議紀要強調該決定與當前高度不確定性的背景相符，
在地緣政治衝突持續時間與範圍難以預判 、 經濟活動放緩步調及其對物價水準影響的訊號錯綜複雜之
下，保持從容並收集額外資訊為適當之舉。Copom 也為下次會議將降息步調加速至 50 個基點保
留空間，但也強調需美伊戰爭等外部環境改善且這些事件對通膨與通膨預期影響有限才能達成。

市場通膨預期方面，截至 3 月 30 日當周巴西央行市場調查顯示，2026 年通膨預期上升 14
個基點至 4.31%，高於巴西央行 3.0% 的長期通膨目標中點，2027、2028 及 2029 年則分別為
3.84%、3.57% 及 3.50%，惟有鑑於近期油價持續走升， 預計未來數週通膨預期將可能進一步惡化。
此外，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 利率中位預期維持在 12.50%，2027 及 2028 年底分別為 10.50%
及 10.00%，顯示雖中東戰爭帶動市場通膨升溫，然而巴西市場整體降息格局並未受到太大影響，仍
是朝大幅降息的方向前進。
另一方面，雖美伊戰爭帶動短線油價上漲，帶動拉美市場通膨預期，然而油價上漲事實上也有利
於拉美整體經濟，主因拉美市場中，包含阿根廷、巴西、哥倫比亞與厄瓜多等國為石油淨出口國，高
盛研究指出若油價上漲 10%，能使整體拉美市場未來 12 個月 GDP 上升約 0.2%，顯示美伊戰爭
對拉美市場來說並非全然只有負面影響。
最後一個值得關注的議題是巴西 2026 年的總統大選，市場主要關注現任總統盧拉，與前總統

波索納洛之子弗拉維奧的對決。市調機構 Quaest 3 月民調結果顯示，在第二輪決選中，兩位候選人
首度打成平手，各獲得 41% 支持度，扭轉過往皆由盧拉勝出的局勢，顯示中東衝突引發的能源價格
上漲與通膨壓力持續影響盧拉政權的支持度 。市場預期若最終總統大選由右派陣營的弗拉維奧勝出，
其政策將會有利於巴西財政赤字問題改善，相較於盧拉政營財政擴張的策略 ，更可能吸引外資流入巴
西市場。
展望後市 ，儘管中東衝突帶動拉美市場通膨預期攀升 ，然而拉美市場最大經濟體巴西仍處於降息
循環中，且目前市場並不預期巴西央行會因為能源價格問題，而扭轉寬鬆方向，此外，拉美市場整體
亦會受惠於油價上漲帶動的石油出口助益，故整體而言，我們目前對拉美市場後市仍抱持正向看法。

能源類股：美伊停戰協商短期難有結論，可以資產配置角度布局能源類股
美伊衝突仍是 3 月主導能源價格走勢的關鍵，雖然美國在 3 月 25 日透過巴基斯坦向伊朗提出停
戰協議，然而內容與伊朗訴求相差甚遠，預料短期內美伊達成停戰協議的可能性並不高，戰事仍可能
需要來回數次的武力攻擊與談判後，才能底定。
隨戰爭持續，荷莫茲海峽的中斷程度仍是油價最關鍵的變數，戰前荷莫茲海峽每天約有 130 艘
船通過海峽 ， 目前每天僅有不到 6 艘在伊朗許可下通行，海峽運輸仍幾乎處於癱瘓狀態 。 對伊朗而言 ，
封鎖荷莫茲海峽越久 ， 越能為其換得更好的談判籌碼 ， 伊朗國會目前更是在推動立法對通過海峽的船

舶收取過路費，以此將海峽視為經濟收入來源，故何時荷莫茲海峽能夠暢通，仍具高度不確定性。
受荷莫茲海峽運量持續受阻影響，高盛 3 月 23 日報告再度上修油價預測，預估 3 月布蘭特原油
平均價格將達 105 美元，4 月將進一步上升至 115 美元，並在 2026 年第四季回落至 80 美元，該預
測基於荷莫茲海峽石油流量將在 6 週內維持極低水準的假設。此外，在海峽流量中斷 10 週的不利情
境下，高盛預估布蘭特油價將可能接近或超越 2008 年 147 美元的歷史紀錄，隨後在2026 年第四季
放緩至 100 美元；在中斷10 週並同時伴隨基礎設施受損的嚴重不利情境下，油價可能飆至接近160
美元，並在2026 年第四季仍維持 115 美元高油價。在高盛各種情境分析中，若海峽流量中斷時間延
長，或者有更多能源基礎設施受損，長期油價都存在上升的可能性，有利能源類股表現。
另一方面，觀察 Bloomberg 西德州原油期貨遠期曲線，可以看出目前市場仍定價荷莫茲海峽受
阻為中短期事件，曲線在開戰後前端快速上揚，反映現貨端供給緊張狀況，然而遠端合約變動幅度不

大，且近幾個月合約曲線陡峭，顯示市場認為荷莫茲海峽受阻仍將是短期事件，並不預期戰爭將會演
變成長期戰事，預料隨衝突結束，原油供給將大幅恢復，帶動油價下跌。3 月 30 日 WTI 遠期曲線顯
示，油價將會在 11 月回落至每桶 80 美元以下。此外，截至 3/30，國際賭盤 Polymarket 預估美伊
在 4/15 前停戰機率約 17%，4/30 前為 32%，6/30 前為 59%，12/31 前則為 75%， 機率在 4/30
至 6/30 區間增幅最大，隱含市場對停火的預期主要落在 4~6 月。
展望後市，雖然近期美伊皆提出談判條件，然而雙方在談判條件上仍存在顯著落差，再加上對於
伊朗來說，將荷莫茲海峽封鎖的越久，讓能源價格維持高檔，越能為伊朗得到更好的談判籌碼，故預
估伊朗更可能採取邊打邊談的策略，將整體談判時程拉長。對於能源類股來說，短線上若戰爭持續，
能源類股有望走出獨立行情，並可作為投資組合中對衝戰爭風險的角色，而即使在停戰後，長期油價
仍有望高於戰前水準，亦有利支撐能源類股表現，故建議可以資產配置角度，適度布局能源類股。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：美伊戰事壓抑短線金價表現，惟反轉條件已逐步成形
黃金傳統上被視為戰爭期間的避險資產，然而本波自 2 月 28 日美伊戰爭爆發以來，金價卻明顯
表現不佳，從戰前約 5,100 美元一路下跌，一度回撤至約 4,100 美元位置。本波金價走疲，主因美伊
戰爭導致荷莫茲海峽受阻，持續推升油價，帶動市場通膨預期上升，同步推高了美國公債殖利率，使
不孳息的黃金資產吸引力下降。此外，市場避險情緒亦帶動美元走強，而黃金價格長期以來與美元走
勢呈負相關，隨美元指數走升，黃金等以美元計價之商品價格亦出現承壓。
此外，黃金重要買盤全球央行，近期購金動能也出現明顯放緩。世界黃金協會報告指出，2026

年 1 月全球央行淨購金量驟降至 5 噸，較去年月均購金量 27 噸銳減近 80%，推測主因 1 月金價波
動過大，導致各國央行態度轉為觀望。 另一方面，土耳其央行近期大幅減持黃金 ，也對市場情緒造
成壓抑。據彭博新聞引述知情人士報導，土耳其央行在 3 月 13 日至 3 月 20 日的兩周內，以出售及
協議交換置換了約 60 噸黃金，用以穩定因美伊戰爭而受影響的土耳其里拉匯率，彭博指出，土耳其
央行本次減持黃金，一部分是採市場直接拋售，一部分則是通過 SWAP 以黃金作為抵押品來獲取外
匯或里拉 ，而歷史上各國央行透過 SWAP 以貴金屬獲得廉價美元融資的情況並不少見，且回顧 2023
年 3 月至 5 月期間，土耳其央行亦曾售出近 160 噸黃金，惟當時因來自黃金 ETF 的買家吸收賣壓，
對短線金價並未造成太大影響，故若僅有來自土耳其央行的一次性賣壓，實際並不需要太過擔憂。
更需要留意的是 ， 中東戰爭將可能迫使其他國家同樣採取出售黃金儲備的動作，以支撐外匯存底
或籌措資金，若此趨勢擴大，將 可能進一步增加黃金短線賣壓， 同時，因目前市場資金較不偏好黃

金，短線上黃金 ETF 吸納來自各國央行賣壓的力道也會較弱，故只要美伊戰爭持續，黃金類股短線
表現將可能受到較大壓力。
即便如此，我們仍可看出世界央行對中長期增持黃金儲備抱持正向態度 ，例如在 1 月份的世界央
行黃金買家中，就意外的出現了馬來西亞央行，為該國自 2018 年以來首度增持黃金儲備，另外在 3
月初，韓國央行也宣布計畫從 2026 年第一季起，將黃金 ETF 納入其外匯準備投資組合，顯示各國
央行對於增持黃金儲備的長期方向仍未改變 。世界黃金協會也預估 ，預期全球央行在 2026 年將會增
持約 850 噸黃金，增持水準將會與去年相近，顯示未來世界央行購金買盤仍可望為金價帶來支撐。
此外，展望後市 ，金價反轉的條件實際上也已在逐步成形，當前市場直覺認為高油價將導致通膨 ，
預期聯準會動作會偏鷹，導致金價表現受到壓抑。然而若戰事在短期內結束，預料市場將忽略短期
油價上漲的影響，使降息預期大致回升到戰前水準，有利金價表現；而若美伊戰爭 進一步擴大，將
可能使油價被推升至壓抑經濟表現的價格，屆時經濟更可能面臨的問題反而不是通膨而是衰退， 聯

準會更可能降息以救經濟，隨利率走低亦有利金價反彈。故 目前來看，隨金價回檔已深，重回多頭
條件也已在逐步成形，建議短線可持續觀望戰事變化，中性看待黃金類股後市表現。

### Page 11

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：通膨疑慮雖推升利率，未來仍有降息可能
FOMC 3/18 會議釋出訊號略偏鷹派。投票結果 11 比 1，僅 Miran 主張降息一碼，而原先偏鴿
的 Waller 轉為支持按兵不動，內部對降息的共識正在弱化。會後聲明雖新增中東局勢不確定性的描
述，釋出政策需保持耐心的訊號，但整體對經濟評估仍維持「穩健」 ，未因2 月就業數據轉弱而顯著
調整措辭。點陣圖中位數雖未變動，但分布結構出現轉變。首先，預期降息的官員人數減少，支持多
次降息的比例下滑；其次，已出現個別官員預期未來升息的情境；此外，長期利率中位數上修，顯示

中性利率評估提高。
雖然因中東能源問題導致 Fed Funds 隔夜利率交換市場所估算今年底前的利率變動預期已從戰
事爆發前的降息 2 碼大幅縮減，近期更出現不排除升息的臆測。然而，自美以伊衝突以來至 3/26(台
北時間)， 包括高盛在內的投資銀行所發布對Fed 利率(區間上緣)預期均認為 FOMC 未來兩年仍將傾
向降息。若此預期得以實現，代表市場目前已過度反映 Fed 的「鷹派情境」，之後殖利率可望有回
穩的空間。
再者，3 月油價上揚，投資人對於未來美國貨幣政策轉向的疑慮升溫，確實也導致了美國甚至是
全球的主要債券市場殖利率上揚。然而，就在市場聚焦於通膨的同時，包括 Pimco、摩根大通和
Columbia Threadneedle Investments 等投資機構 3/30 不約而同表達，投資人低估了美國對伊朗
戰爭導致經濟急劇放緩的風險的看法，並認為這場衝擊最終將引發債券殖利率再度回落 。高盛 3 月下
旬的報告提醒，比起升息，中東戰事更大的風險在於(接近)經濟衰退，這可能觸發 Fed 更大幅度的降

息。Pimco 首席投資官 Daniel Ivascyn 也表示「通常始於通膨衝擊的局面，會迅速演變為成長衝擊…
而我們正處於經濟大幅走軟的邊緣。」因此，未來債券殖利率不排除有向下的可能性存在。
隨著美國 11 月期中選舉逼近，川普在政治上將面臨更大的壓力，以美國財政體質及川普向來的
政策來看，之後美國政府勢必將盡力壓低美國公債的殖利率，有利使美債利率處於相對可控的範圍。
近期美國公債殖利率雖因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目標(2%)，
美債的高息收對長線投資人具強大誘因 ， 因此投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置 。 此
外，包括 Pimco、摩根大通等投資機構也認為未來債券殖利率有向下的可能性存在。以目前的殖利
率看，美債仍有投資空間。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策預期變動影響較大，故在年期
與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 總經與雙率: Warsh談話, 繼續淡化前瞻指引
1. Warsh談話: 現階段屬於觀察其風格的時間窗口, 演說繼續淡化前瞻指引, 對升降息不透口風。
2. 非農與雙率: 1)美國雙率- 非農溫和下升息機率不高, 但市場預期仍讓殖利率續處高位震盪, 也支
持美元指數; 2)FX- Warsh立場偏好縮表、回收游資, 加上美國雙率高位, 持續對亞幣形成壓抑。
• 產業與股市: 上下游利潤拉扯, 但上肥下瘦情況不變
1. 短線: 「 Meta出租算力、硬體漲價外溢」增添市場擾動, 加上短線乖離過大, 引發股市修正。
2. 長線: 算力需求未歇、記憶體報價持續上揚, AI產業多頭的趨勢未變, 科技股拉回仍可分批布局。
2. 後續看點
• Warsh國會證詞(7/15)、台積電第二季法說會(7/16)、FOMC會議(7/29) 、FIFA世界盃7月20日3:00。
策略思維
1. 股市- 多頭格局, 但留意市場波動: 獲利動能穩健下, 現階段泡沫擔憂尚低, 長線多頭格局未變。不過, 技術面

乖離率高, 融資、槓桿活動熱, 確實增添短線市場波動, 有拉回可視為買進機會。
2. 債市- 聯準會保持觀望, 利率偏高震盪: 油價走向有助通膨走降, 就業穩定下, 10年美債殖利率區間走勢未變,
可站現金流考量布局投資等級債券, 並以中短天期為先。
3. 匯市- 亞幣延續區間格局: 1)美元指數高位震盪、2)美國雙率高位下, 持續對亞幣形成壓抑。

年會重要發言 市場利率路徑預期(至2026/12)
• 本屆Sintra論壇關注的主要央行態度, 以及對於美伊情勢緩和後的總經判斷。BoE Bailey承認能源衝擊將使英國通
膨上升至3.2%, 但應先觀察能源成本是否進一步傳導, 而非急於透過升息回應。ECB Lagarde則解釋6月升息的判
斷原因, 並強調未來仍依賴數據、不預測政策路徑。市場對歐、英升息預期已較先前降溫。
• 市場更關注Warsh的態度, 整體延續6月FOMC會議立場: 1)不提供前瞻指引; 2)重申央行獨立性; 3)重視通膨目標,
儘管目前物價仍高, 但坦言過去四週通膨風險已有所降溫。
• Warsh的省話風格, 使市場對政策訊號更看重美國經濟數據, 而非Fed的前瞻預測。導致現況上, 即便目前油價下行,
但美國CPI年增率仍在4%上方, 影響市場對Fed升息預期仍高、美元雙率延續強勢的主因。

6月非農低於預期, 而失業率下滑 JOLTS: 職缺/失業比回升, 勞動市場流動性維持均衡
資料來源: Bloomberg。 註: 雇用(hires)與職位流出(separations, 涵蓋自主離職、轉職、退休)。
資料來源: Bloomberg。
就業｜新增非農放緩、職缺回升, 勞動市場維持供需均衡
• 美國6月非農新增5.7萬低於預期, 且4、5月數據合計下修7.4萬, 緩和市場升息預期。從產業面觀察, 休閒與餐旅減
少6.1萬, 資訊業亦反映科技業持續精簡人力, 為非農降溫主因。失業率下滑至4.2%, 但部分隱含勞參率下滑。
• JOLTS職缺數上升至759萬, 高於市場預期(730萬)。然而, 從勞動市場流動性觀察, 5月雇用動能未同步升溫(企業
擴編態度審慎)、職位流出則有所增加(人員流動持續), 而非就業過熱跡象。
• 後續應留意, 1)世足賽的短期支撐淡化後, 2)在導入AI後, 企業的用人結構朝效率導向調整, 均可能為就業動能帶來
不確定性。考量就業均衡狀態能否延續、薪資成長延續偏降溫趨勢, 就業對通膨的壓力亦將減緩。

Cathay’s View
市場維持升息預期
資料來源: Bloomberg。 資料來源: Bloomberg。
• 美伊達成和平協議、荷姆茲海峽通行量升高, 帶動油價跌至70美元附近, 有助緩和通膨預期。短線因: 1)部份Fed
官員偏鷹, 2)預估第三季通膨壓力仍存(通膨約在3%), 3)部分就業數據優於預期。上述因素導致市場維持年底升息
預期, 導致美元維持百元關卡上方, 短率亦同步反彈, 使得殖利率曲線趨平(10Y-2Y≈30bps)。
• 後續變化: 1)短期內關注市場政策預期變化, 若未來市場升息預期緩和, 整體殖利率曲線將有下移空間 。2)中長期
而言, 除了貨幣政策走勢外, 仍需考量Fed縮表影響; 長天期利率(10~30年)仍將受財政紀律、QT隱含流動性趨緊的
背景下, 研判殖利率曲線仍偏向陡峭。

Cathay’s View
2022年美元利差收斂, 東北亞貨幣走貶 美元利率與東北亞貨幣之相關性
資料來源: Bloomberg。
註: 資料時間為2015/1/1-2026/6/30。以10Y美債與日圓(USDJPY)的正相關(0.9)為例, 若利
率越高, 則日圓(USDJPY)數值越大, 即日圓偏向貶值。
資料來源: Bloomberg。
• 2021年起長短天期美債利差(10年減2年)快速收斂, 並導致東北亞貨幣(日圓、韓元、新台幣)大幅走貶。FOMC會
後, 利差再度快速收斂, 是否將令前述貨幣重演貶勢?
• 回顧美元利差與各貨幣過去10年走勢, 確實呈現弱至中等負相關(美元利差收斂, 貨幣偏向貶值)。然而, 若進一步
拆解利差, 長天期美債利率與各貨幣呈現更高的相關性(利率上升, 貨幣偏向貶值), 為影響匯價的主因。
• 因此, 後續長天期美債利率波動方向, 實為關鍵。考量市場升息預期居高不下, 引導利率徘徊於波動區間上緣, 意味
短期內各貨幣恐延續區間震盪甚至偏弱的走勢。

東北亞貨幣｜ 美元利率居高, 增添資金流出誘因, 東北亞貨幣料延續區間偏弱走勢

日本壽險業者各類資產比重 美元利率居高不下, 將令日圓承壓
資料來源: Bloomberg。 資料來源: Bloomberg。
• 本週日圓貶破162關卡, 創下40年新低, 與此同時, 日債中長天期利率當週上揚10-15bps, 或反映債市再度遭逢減碼、
資金向外流出的情勢, 與去年底以來, 日本壽險業者持續減碼日債、加碼海外債的趨勢一致, 主要反映日債前景仍
不明朗(BOJ升息終點不明、高市政府大舉擴張財政), 成為匯市的一大影響因子。
• 另一方面, 市場對於Fed年內升息的預期居高不下, 引導美元利率徘徊高檔, 增添資金流出誘因, 以及財務省遲遲未
進場干預, 間接助長貶值預期, 意味匯價帶來壓力。
• 展望後市, 日圓若欲扭轉偏弱格局, 有待1)BOJ釋出更加鷹派的訊號, 或2)Fed升息預期降溫、美元利率回降, 或3)
債市信心回籠。在此之前, 日圓料延續偏弱格局。惟需留意財務省進場干預的可能性持續提高, 將放大日圓波動。
日圓｜ 日債轉弱、美元利率居高, 增添日圓偏弱壓力

Cathay’s View
Meta出租算力, 不影響記憶體需求強勁狀況 AI agent來臨, Token需求呈指數型成長
股市｜Meta出租算力是改善使用率問題, 與算力供給過剩問題無關
• 傳聞Meta 正在規劃雲端基礎設施業務 , 把多餘的 AI 算力與模型開放給外部客戶使用 , 就像Amazon  AWS 、
Microsoft Azure、Google Cloud 的部分模式, 市場的直覺反應是, AI算力有供給過剩的問題, 同時, 原本享受AI算力
紅利的公司, 股價紛紛重挫, 如Nebius重挫近-17%、CoreWeave跌-14%。其實, 這還在規劃中, 且是為了改善基礎
建設利用率的方法之一, 而非供給過剩問題。
• 從韓國的出口及記憶體報價來看, 目前還是持續攀升, 顯示AI需求仍舊強勁, 此外, 市場對AI token的需求量來看,
呈現指數型的成長, 顯示供需缺口仍舊非常大。

Cathay’s View
乖離過大市場, 短線調整壓力大 除科技、能源外 ,  高油價衝擊其他各類股獲利動能
股市｜費半、韓國乖離過大, 短線回調, 但科技獲利動能持續走強, 拉回後仍視為布局契機
• 蘋果有意採購中國長鑫晶片、海力士有意生產傳統記憶體等訊息影響, 導致費半指數一度回落近10%, 科技股也同
步走弱。與此同時, 資金轉向健護、公用事業等防禦類股避險, 主因為費半及科技股先前漲幅已大, 乖離過大, 市場
一旦出現利空訊息, 容易引發投資人獲利了結。另一方面, 健護類股目前評價相對合理, 加上國際間伊波拉病毒疫
情升溫, 帶動市場對疫苗相關概念股的關注, 使資金短暫流入防禦型及題材型類股。
• 就獲利而言, 科技產業仍是未來市場主流方向不變。受惠於輝達新一代晶片出貨升溫, 預期第二季科技業獲利成長
動能將進一步增強, 從53%提高至63%。反觀其他類股, 受到國際油價先前大幅波動影響, 獲利表現呈現下修狀態,
甚至不如Q1。因此, 科技或費半指數的後續拉回, 仍可視為分批布局的契機。

iPhone存儲占比由9%升至27% 消費信心與耐久財購買意願
資料來源: WSJ預估。 資料來源: Bloomberg。
• 市場預期新一代iPhone 18漲價, 觀察整體消費信心與耐久財購買意願, 目前市場呈現「心理謹慎、資金分流」的
格局。前者蹣跚走低, 後者節節敗退, 隱含民眾對於大筆開支的評估期拉長。整體上, 對蘋果ASP或營收可能壓力
有限, 但對觀察美國中產階級消費力而言, 高單價指標型消費耐久財的價格彈性, 則是一個重要的壓力測試指標。
• 對經濟動能來看, 結合信用卡拖欠餘額比例接近金融海嘯之際 , 第四季整體零售銷售預期將呈現依舊明顯的 「K型
分流」。高階3C產品與剛性科技需求依然具備品牌黏著度 , 但可能會對其餘非必需消費品 （如服飾、家居修繕）
產生局部排擠。

今年以來, 美中科技股走勢皆極度分化 上下游獲利呈現上肥下瘦
美股｜上肥下瘦」的現象下半年仍將延續, 上游硬體股還是佔優勢
• 2026年上半, AI產業上下游分化明顯, 而且這個現象並不只侷限於美股, 太平洋彼岸的中港股也是如此。主因在於
成本與需求傳導不對稱。上游晶片因技術壁壘高、供不應求, 享有強定價權與高毛利; 下游CSP雖大舉投資數據中
心, 但面臨採購成本上漲、競爭激烈及AI應用變現緩慢, 成本快速上升卻難以轉嫁的情況, 也因而壓縮獲利空間。
• 後市而言, 「上肥下瘦」的現象下半年仍將延續, 因為上游新平台出貨高峰, 成長動能可望維持至年底。下游需待
AI殺手級應用落地(如物流全自動化、手機等終端裝置跳躍性進步)、ROI改善後才有望反轉。整體而言, AI長期趨勢仍舊穩健, 今
年下半還是偏重具定價權的上游供應商, 下游CSP廠則需密切觀察獲利走向、殺手級應用的出現。

台積電CoWoS產能加速擴產 台積電大舉擴產, 將支持明年營收成長三成以上
資料來源:Morgan Stanley預估。 資料來源: Morgan Stanley, J.P. Morgan, Bloomberg預估。
• 台積電法說會前, 多間外資上調台積電目標價到3000元以上, 對台積電股價形成支持。由於AI需求強勁, AI硬體漲
價潮將推升台積電毛利率展望, 此外, 台積電大舉擴產以滿足客戶需求, 使外資上調台積電明年CoWoS預估產能,
上調幅度達21%, 支持台積電明年營收的成長空間。
• 台積電上季法說會表示, 預估未來數年的營收增速將持續高於資本支出增速 , 若今明年資本支出分別達560億美元、

750億美元, 兩年資本支出成長均超過30%, 則今年營收年增有望接近40%, 明年營收仍維持30%以上的高成長。
台股｜台積電產能供不應求, 同步推高毛利率與營收成長, 今明年營收成長挑戰三成以上

科技股Q2領漲大盤 單月最大跌幅有擴大趨勢
資料來源: Bloomberg。
註: 以當月最低點與前一月收盤價計算。
資料來源: Bloomberg。
• 日經225指數Q2大漲38.5%, 但除了科技股大漲113.9%之外, 其他各類股皆落後大盤, 顯示產業分化情形明顯。除
了科技、金融、原物料、通訊等受到高油價影響較小之外, 其他族群則普遍承壓。
• 日經自2023/1以來已走了3年半多頭, 雖然基本面改善支撐股市持續上揚, 但漲多之後修正幅度亦又快又急, 單月出
現雙位數跌幅的次數增加, 短線波動風險也隨之升高 。中東情勢降溫有助於先前受到高油價衝擊的企業營運好轉 ,
指數仍有上漲空間, 但由於漲勢集中在科技股且累積較大漲幅, 指數波動恐加劇, 可採取分批方式布局。

油價回落僅能暫時抑制短期貶勢 印度、印尼、菲律賓長期為雙赤字國家
新興亞幣｜油價回落使原先疲弱的新興亞幣獲得喘息, 然長期貶值壓力仍存
• 隨近期美伊和談有所進展帶動油價回落, 今年表現疲弱的新興亞幣出現反彈, 因上述國家能源對外依存度較高, 油
價上行會對其貿易帳產生壓力。
• 然而, 雖短線的貶勢暫緩, 但以上貨幣長線貶值因素仍尚未解除。印度、印尼、菲律賓長期均為雙赤字國家, 財政
體質較為羸弱, 且不具備大規模的出口創匯能力 。又近期外資對於東南亞 、南亞的投資明顯縮手 (如印度的FDI與
FPI同步下行), 以上因素使貶值壓力始終籠罩新興亞幣。
資料來源: Bloomberg。 資料來源: Bloomberg。

写评论
图⽚
可灵 AI ⼀轮近 30 亿美元的融资接近完成。此轮投资后，可灵投后估值有望达到 180 亿美
元。此次融资，可灵 AI 创下全球视频⼤模型公司最⼤额融资纪录。本轮融资由 CPE 源峰、
国⽅创投、 BlueFive 、腾讯、中关村科学城基⾦、中信证券联合领投，以及数⼗家⼀线机
构共同参与。
摘要：
7 ⽉ 2 ⽇晚间，快⼿发布公告，宣布可灵 AI ⼀轮近 30 亿美元的融资接近完成。
据公告，公司主体北京可灵当⽇与 21 名初始投资者签署协议，投资者将约 138 亿元⼈⺠币（约合
20.28 亿美元）的现⾦注⼊北京可灵，后者重组后将承接快⼿集团可灵 AI 相关资产及业务。
同时，该协议约定，在认购期内，还有 15 名额外投资者将以 52 亿元⼈⺠币（约合 7.67 亿美元）

的现⾦注⼊北京可灵。
根据协议，北京可灵还预留了额外认购窗⼝：⾃协议签署⽇起 60 天内（或经持超 50% 认购额投
资者同意可延⻓），新增投资者可通过签署加⼊协议参与本轮融资，但全体投资者累计增资总额
不得超过 204.471 亿元⼈⺠币（约 30 亿美元），对应北京可灵经扩⼤注册资本的 16.67% 。加⼊协
议将作为增资协议的组成部分。
据悉，此轮投资后，可灵投后估值有望达到 180 亿美元。此次融资，可灵 AI 创下全球视频⼤模型
公司最⼤额融资纪录，标志着其独⽴商业化发展进程正式开启。
本轮融资由 CPE 源峰、国⽅创投、 BlueFive 、腾讯、中关村科学城基⾦（联合国科投资）、中信
证券联合领投，以及数⼗家⼀线机构共同参与。该轮融资汇聚阿⾥云、百度等顶级产业资本，
华策影视和芒果产业投资⼈（厚为资本）等头部⽂娱产业⽅积极参与。
本轮融资彰显市场对快⼿ AI 资产的强烈兴趣，募集资⾦预计将⽤于可灵 AI 业务的技术研发与⽣态
拓展。快⼿⽅⾯表⽰，此次引⼊多元战略投资者，有助于加速 AI ⼤模型在视频⽣成等领域的商业

化落地。
本⽂来源：上海证券报

주: K-IFRS연결요약재무제표
[투자포인트]
■ 직원들에 대한 영업이익 10% 지급 조건을 반영한 동사 FY26 영업이익은 261조원으로 YoY 452% 증가할
전망이며 이에 기반한 예상 FY26 BPS는 48만원. CY26 중에 동사 ADR이 상장될 경우 최근 Micron의 P/B
배수가 7~10배 수준 (ROE 71%)이고, 동사의 올해 예상 ROE가 96%에 달하므로 동사에 충분히 7.0배 이상을
적용할 수 있음. P/B 7.5배를 FY26 예상 BPS에 적용 시 350만원의 주가 가능

■ 절대적 Valuation 관점에서도 이번 상승 싸이클의 시작이었던 FY24의 ROE 31%와 고점 P/B 배수 2.4배를
감안 시, FY26의 ROE가 96%라면 7.5배의 P/B 배수 적용이 가능. CY27의 메모리 가격 상승 지속 여부에 따라
동사 주가가 350만원 이상으로 상승할 수 있을지가 결정될 전망
■ 동사 2Q26 영업이익을 63.7조원으로 전망함. DRAM, NAND 출하 증가율은 9%, 18%, Blended ASP
상승률은 37%, 52%를 기록할 것으로 추정됨. 동사의 높은 HBM 판매 비중에 따라 2Q26 DRAM Blended
ASP 상승률이 경쟁사 대비 다소 낮을 가능성이 있는 것으로 보임
■ 메모리 반도체 업황은 적어도 CY27까지는 호황이 이어질 것으로 판단됨. CY27에는 DRAM 업계 생산 증가율이
올해의 25%에서 20% 이하로 하락하고, 빅테크 업체들이 Cash 부족에도 불구하고 유상 증자, SPV & JV 등의

방식으로 데이터센터 투자를 강하게 지속할 전망이기 때문
■ 최근 급등에 따른 단기 주가 조정이 나타날 수 있으나, 업황 및 실적 호조가 지속되고 글로벌 유동성 증감률의
상승이 2H26에 재개될 전망이므로 동사 주가의 중기 상승세는 유효할 것으로 판단됨

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

AI IPOs 101: why they’ll change 
the boom forever

1 
Authors 
Adrian Cox 
Research Analyst

July 01, 2026 
Four reasons why the expected IPOs of OpenAI and Anthropic 
matter so much 
The expected wave of initial public offerings of the world’s leading AI companies 
will change the AI boom forever. 
It will bring unprecedented transparency, funding and accountability to the 
industry – as well as finally giving investors a pure-play benchmark to value the 
likely winners and losers of a revolutionary technology. 
The news at the end of last week that OpenAI is considering delaying its own 
share sale, originally expected for later this year, indicates just how much is at 
stake. The company, which lit the fuse for the AI frenzy with its launch of ChatGPT 
in November 2022, as well as its now slightly larger rival Anthropic, which makes 
Claude, confidentially filed for IPOs in early June. Those share sales would 
reportedly aim to raise about $60bn apiece and value each company at more 
than $1 trillion. 
They and their peers need to strike while the iron is hot to secure computing 
power, distribution and capital. But there is no guarantee that there will be 
enough investor appetite to absorb hundreds of billions of dollars in rapid-fire 
demand. 
The AI boom is fast approaching a moment of truth, as rapid growth and soaring 
valuations collide with ballooning capital expenditure, a public backlash and the 
challenges of real-life adoption. Challenges abound, including Chinese open-
source models that promise similar performance for a fraction of the price. 
Whoever comes out on top could define the era. 
In this report, we look at: 
▪ Why have an IPO? 
▪ Which AI-related companies are likely to have IPOs? 
▪ OpenAI and Anthropic in a nutshell 
▪ Four ways that IPOs will change the AI boom forever 
1. Greater transparency for financials – and business models 
2. More diverse funding sources – and more spending 
3. Greater accountability for their actions – and the industry 
4. New era for comparing valuations to peers – and the past

### Page 2

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
Why have an IPO?
1.  Access new sources of funding  to invest, reduce debt and improve
financial flexibility.
2.  Create liquidity to give existing investors and employees a way to cash out
and enable acquisitions with publicly traded shares.
3.  Improve public credibility and profile , lending additional weight with
consumers, businesses and lawmakers.
Which AI-related companies are likely to have IPOs?

The two leading contenders to sell shares to the public this year are Anthropic, given
a 78 percent chance, according to bets on Polymarket, and OpenAI, now down to
28 percent.
Other AI-related companies that may go public include data analytics platform
Databricks, enterprise data platform Glean, and compliance tool Vanta, according
to Polymarket bets. Others include business process platform Celonis; graphic
design tool Canva; finance operations platform Ramp; defence company Anduril;

and Europe’s only notable frontier-model maker Mistral.
Figure 1: Implied probability of select AI-related IPOs by 2027

Anthropic OpenAI Databricks Glean Vanta Celonis Canva Ramp Anduril Mistral
Source : Polymarket, Deutsche Bank Research
OpenAI in a nutshell
n San Francisco-based OpenAI was founded in 2015 as a non-profit research
laboratory led by Co-Chairs Elon Musk and now-CEO Sam Altman , the
former head of Y-Combinator. It was backed by some of the biggest

investors in Silicon Valley, including Peter Thiel and Reid Hoffman. (Musk
severed ties with OpenAI in 2018, triggering a legal dispute that concluded
in May in OpenAI’s favour.)
n Its mission was “to ensure that artificial general intelligence – AI systems
that are generally smarter than humans – benefits all of humanity”.
n On November 30, 2022, OpenAI launched ChatGPT, a generative AI
chatbot based on its GPT-3.5 large language model, which became a

### Page 3

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
runaway success with 100 million users in three months. GPT-4, released in
March 2023, was so far ahead of the competition that it took a year or more
for serious challengers to gain a foothold. However, GPT-5, launched last
August, was quickly matched amid stiffer competition.
n The non-profit board voted to remove Altman as CEO in November 2023. He
was reinstated four days later, after a revolt by investors and employees.

n Its most important strategic relationship has been with Microsoft. When
OpenAI transitioned into a “capped-profit” entity in 2019, it was able to
obtain capital investment including $1bn from Microsoft, which later added
another $10bn in early 2023. Their exclusive partnership and revenue-
sharing agreements enabled Microsoft to be an early leader in AI and
secured funding and computing power for OpenAI.
n The companies scaled back their partnership in late April 2026, though

Microsoft retains a 27 percent stake in OpenAI. (OpenAI’s for-profit LLC
became a public benefit corporation controlled by the non-profit in May
2025.)
n Today, more than one in ten people on the planet use ChatGPT every
week. The name is to chatbots what PostIts are to sticky labels. It reportedly
surpassed $25bn in annualised revenue in March and has indicated to
investors that it will achieve its first annual profit in 2030.
n Although the market is increasingly crowded, OpenAI is still producing

cutting-edge models, with GPT-5.5 excelling at agentic coding. Brand new
GPT-5.6 won the same badge of honour as the latest Claude model last
Friday by being restricted to a small group of users over US government
concerns that it was so powerful that it could pose a security risk. It also has
a strong developer ecosystem building on top of its Application
Programming Interface (API).
Anthropic in a nutshell
n San Francisco-based Anthropic was founded in 2021  by seven former

employees of OpenAI, including Dario Amodei, its CEO , and his sister
Daniela Amodei, its president. Anthropic is a public benefit corporation
whose mission is “to ensure that the world safely makes the transition
through transformative AI”.
n Led by its Claude models, the company has focused heavily on the
enterprise market, positioning itself as a bastion of high standards of safety
and reliability.
n Google parent Alphabet and Amazon have invested billions of dollars in

Anthropic. Like OpenAI, it has struck complex agreements that commit it to
use chips and data centres from its investors in return, including a
commitment reported by The Information to spend about $200bn with
Google Cloud over five years.
n This has been a breakout year for Anthropic. The release of its Cowork and
Claude Code agent tools in January and February prompted a
“SaaSpocalypse” slump in stocks of software-as-a-service companies,
amid concerns that their utility-like business models could be largely

disintermediated.
n The Claude website had almost one billion visits in May , still behind
ChatGPT at 5.6 billion and Google’s Gemini at nearly three billion, but way
up from 200 million in January, according to data from Similarweb.
Meanwhile, Claude Code now accounts for slightly more than half of the
business-focused coding market.
n Anthropic’s revenue reached an annual recurring rate of $47bn in May,

### Page 4

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
overtaking OpenAI. CEO Amodei said that an 80-fold increase in usage in
the first quarter on an annualised basis was “just crazy” and “too hard to
handle,” forcing it to limit access for some users and sign up new sources
of compute to keep up with demand.
n A disagreement with the US Administration came out into the open in
February when the Department of Defense announced that it was a supply-

chain risk. The company had demanded restrictions on the use of its
technology for domestic surveillance and autonomous weapons.
n Then two weeks ago, Anthropic abruptly switched off its most powerful
Fable 5 and Mythos 5 models worldwide after the US Department of
Commerce ordered it to restrict access by foreigners, due to concerns that
it could be weaponised by bad actors. It won approval to restore some
access at the end of last week.
Four ways that upcoming IPOs will change the AI boom

forever
1. Greater transparency for financials and business models
Nothing sharpens an entrepreneur’s mind – and business model – like the
prospect of an IPO.
n It is a decision that they may take with some trepidation, given the
obligation to open the doors and explain their finances, business model and
outlook in detail.
To prepare for an IPO, AI companies will have to produce public-company-grade
reporting, credible performance indicators, risk analyses and forecasts.

and corporate governance.
n Remuneration may also have to be revamped, with public metrics around
pay for performance for management and provisions for employees to vest
stock.
Greater transparency may help investors take a view on how much they consider
currently lossmaking AI models to be winner-takes-all, like Amazon or Meta, where
network effects create a virtuous circle, or commodities, where one model is
viewed as interchangeable with another.

n While OpenAI has the leading chatbot by far with around 900 million
monthly users, it has been rapidly pivoting its business model this year to
try to attract enterprise users who will pay for it.
n That includes creating the OpenAI Deployment Company in May with 19
private-equity groups led by TPG to distribute its enterprise products,
spearheaded by so-called “forward deployed engineers”. It said previously
that enterprise revenue was on track to match consumer revenue by the end

of the year.
n Since CEO Sam Altman declared a “code red” in December, OpenAI has
scrapped what it called “side quests” such as its video generating tool Sora
and is working on a “unified superapp” that brings its chatbot, coding,
browsing and agentic capabilities together, aiming to convert satisfied
home users to a similar business offering.

### Page 5

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
n Anthropic overtook it in business subscriptions in May, according to data
from Ramp. And Similarweb data shows monthly visits to ChatGPT fell
below a majority of the generative AI market for the first time in May,
suggesting consumers are increasingly willing to switch between models.

and mobile web)

n OpenAI has indicated that it is generating about $25bn to $33bn in
annualised revenue, up from a $4bn annual rate at the end of 2024. By
comparison, Anthropic’s annualised run rate shot up from $1bn a year at

and public hyperscalers rely on AI models for just a small part of their AI
revenues.
n The key questions are how intense and rapid adoption will be; is the return
on investment justified once businesses have to make hard choices to scale
beyond pilots; and will premium all-singing, all-dancing frontier models win
out over cheap, stripped-down, task-specific alternatives?
The stakes for pure-play model makers are higher now than ever before, with

pressure from above and below.
n On the one hand, the hyperscalers have unsurpassed access to data,
distribution and compute, as well as the ability to offer competing models
on their platforms and still take a cut.
n On the other hand, ever-smarter open-source models may be good enough
for most tasks. GLM 5.2, an open-source model from China’s Z.ai, has
become a viral hit in the US after rocketing up the leaderboards over the past
couple of weeks. It scores 51 in Artificial Analysis’s Intelligence Index, not

### Page 6

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
far behind Claude’s (currently restricted) Fable 5 and OpenAI’s GPT-5.5, yet
costs just 48 cents per task in the index, versus $2.75 and $1.03
respectively.
Price is becoming a battleground as enterprise customers begin to balk at the
true costs of rolling AI out at scale.
n Yes, cost per token, the building block of AI, is falling by between nine and
900 times a year, depending on the task, according to Epoch AI. But that is

being offset by ever more compute-hungry agentic applications – as well as
by well-meaning initiatives to encourage employees to be creative that
have incentivised budget-busting “token-maxxing”.
n The economics of AI suddenly look less attractive once token usage is no
longer being subsidised by investors. Anthropic has changed from a flat fee
to a usage-based model, and OpenAI’s Altman said last month that cost had
come out of the blue to become a “huge issue” for customers this year. Both

are reported to be considering price cuts that could erode their profit
margins.

n OpenRouter data showing weekly token usage of the top models on its
platform indicates that the number of tokens processed by US models has
risen fivefold this year to 20 trillion, convincingly outpaced by a thirteenfold

increase by Chinese models to 26 trillion. The platform allows customers to
access hundreds of LLMs from different providers through a single
account.

n Amazon, Walmart and Cisco are among companies that have reined in
token use. Uber burned through its AI budget for the year in four months and

has now capped spending, with its Chief Operating Officer saying that “the
link is not there yet” between the use of Claude Code and customer
innovation.
n Demand is growing for new model architectures that can divert queries to
the most efficient model for any given prompt, such as OpenRouter’s new
Fusion plugin.
2. More diverse funding sources – and more spending
AI is an arms race for compute, pitting startups against established hyperscalers.

n Specialist model-makers are on the back foot compared with a company
like Alphabet parent Google that has ample cloud compute facilities and is
generating roughly $50bn in cash from operations every quarter that it can
use to buy even more.
n Private companies cannot tap the same pool of investors forever. Abundant
public market funding would break the cycle of relying on the piecemeal
multi-billion-dollar investments and complicated financing arrangements

among the leading firms of the past few months.
To be sure, this has not been a bad time to be raising private money.
n Three lean years for venture investment reversed last year as investors
injected $425bn, an increase of almost a third from the previous year, into
more than 24,000 companies, Crunchbase data show. AI funding was
particularly healthy, exceeding $225bn globally, albeit concentrated in few

### Page 8

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
companies, according to CB Insights.

fewer companies: quarterly equity funding ($bn) and deal count

Source : CB Insights, Deutsche Bank Research

n Anthropic said at the end of May that it raised $65bn at a post-money
valuation of $965bn, more than double its valuation in February. In one leap,
it overtook OpenAI, which raised $122bn at an $852bn post-money
valuation in March. If either achieved the mooted market value of $1trn, it
would be comfortably within the world’s top 20 most valuable companies.
But growth does not come cheap.
n OpenAI told investors in February that it was targeting some $600bn in

infrastructure investments by 2030, less than half of its earlier estimate of
$1.4tn amid concerns about whether future revenue could justify the
outlay. Its emphasis has changed from building its own data centres to just
renting them.
n OpenAI’s funding round in March was anchored by long-term partners
Amazon, Nvidia and SoftBank. Amazon will invest $50bn in OpenAI in
return for OpenAI using 2GW of its Trainium chip capacity and cooperating
on new joint products.

n Long-term shareholder and partner Microsoft still took part even though
the two have grown more distant and unwound some exclusive
arrangements in April. The startup also took investments from at least 19
institutional investors and funds, as well as some retail investors via banks
and exchange-traded funds, and expanded a revolving credit facility to
about $4.7bn.
n Meanwhile, Anthropic’s latest round looped in at least two dozen investors
and included $15bn of previously committed investments from

hyperscalers including $5bn from Amazon – after the startup agreed to buy
up to 5GW of compute capacity from it and a similar amount from Google
and Broadcom.
n In a sign of just how complicated the AI funding org chart is becoming,
Google, which already reportedly owned a 14 percent stake in Anthropic

### Page 9

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
worth $3bn, pledged to invest another $40bn in it in April and more recently
was said by Bloomberg to have agreed to backstop lease payments worth
$35bn on five data centres.
IPOs would not just unlock new equity finance.
n Public listings would give the companies and their backers leverage
capacity: the ability to raise debt against liquid, transparently valued
collateral. Those funds could then be recycled into further expenditure on

data centres, acquisitions and other ventures.
n A publicly funded OpenAI or Anthropic would likely not just build bigger
models but also seek to become the default foundational platform layer
similar to what Windows was for PCs and to Amazon’s AWS for the cloud.
They could use larger war chests to expand their API services, developer
tools and enterprise partnerships, commoditising the layers where rivals
(and perhaps some customers) operate.
Financial flexibility matters for all of the players in the land grab for capacity in the

AI boom.
n Capital expenditure by hyperscalers looks set to rise by two-thirds to more
than $750bn this year. Even cash-rich Google turned to the debt markets
this year, raising almost $32bn in less than 24 hours in February, then raising
$84.75bn in the biggest equity capital markets transaction ever on June 1.
Nvidia sold $25bn of high-grade bonds in mid-June.
n And smaller companies are turning to the public markets, most recently
with South Korean memory chip maker SK Hynix announcing that it is

planning to raise more than 45 trillion won, or almost $30bn, in a landmark
US listing.
An opening of the IPO floodgates after a five-year drought would give companies
the currency they need to embark on a spending spree.
n It could even break the inertia of an upstream drought in fundraising for
early investments by unblocking trapped liquidity. Global venture capital
fundraising fell last year to $122 billion, little more than a quarter of the peak

in 2021 and 2022, as investors shied away from illiquid investments in
favour of less risky strategies, PitchBook data show.

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 10 Deutsche Bank AG
Figure 6: Global private-equity fundraising dropped significantly after 2022,
trapping upstream liquidity

n That could mean a surge in mergers and acquisitions, turning other private

companies into targets and giving an exit route to impatient investors. This
year has already seen more than 5,000 technology acquisitions or
investments pending or completed with a value of about $850bn, more than
double the level for the same period last year, Bloomberg data show.
n Elevated valuations and regulatory hurdles for the biggest companies
would put them out of reach of all but a tiny number of strategic buyers (the
hyperscalers) or sovereigns. An alternative approach has been

“acquihires”, pioneered by Microsoft two years ago when it hired most of
the staff of Inflection, including co-founder Mustafa Suleyman, and
licensed the use of its AI models for $650 million.
n But smaller companies have a much greater chance of being acquired.
n Most recently, US chipmaker Onemi agreed to buy Synaptics, which makes
semiconductors for smart devices, for about $6.2bn in stock late last week.
Indeed, Bending Spoons, which buys up struggling software companies,

including AOL and Vimeo, itself filed for an IPO last week. The Italian
company aims to raise as much as $1.6bn at a $19bn valuation early this
month.
Last but not least, IPOs will give companies a potent new weapon in the battle for
talent: liquid, publicly traded stock options.
n For top-tier researchers and engineers, this would be a far more tangible
and immediate form of wealth than illiquid shares in a private rival that
might seek to lure them away.

n Such a liquidity shock could force private competitors into responding with
ever larger funding rounds at higher valuations on the promise of “jam
tomorrow” for star employees – or into going public themselves. They
would also have to be on high alert to prevent their existing core teams from
being poached by a newly public OpenAI or Anthropic armed with a trillion-
dollar market capitalisation.
n Meta’s Mark Zuckerberg sparked a bidding war for top AI researchers last

### Page 11

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 11
year, offering compensation packages worth hundreds of millions of
dollars in some cases. OpenAI and Anthropic were the most recent players
to fish in the small pond of genuine AI experts a couple of weeks ago,
hooking two legends of the industry. Shares of Google, where they worked,
fell more than 5 percent on the day.
3. Greater accountability for their actions – and for the industry
The AI industry has lost its lustre in recent months.

n Warm initial perceptions of it as a slightly magical composer of wedding
speeches have faded and may vanish once Mom and Pop own shares in
pure-play AI companies for the first time. Those companies risk becoming
a lightning rod for broader fears about the impact of AI on trust, jobs and
vulnerable industries.
At the most basic level, public companies are held to higher standards around
consistency and accountability.
n For example, OpenAI has attracted criticism for reversing its earlier

opposition to advertising in an attempt to create a more sustainable
business model. Critics have said conversations could be biased by sales
imperatives.
Beyond that, OpenAI and Anthropic have already had a taste of the political
challenges in store as public companies.
n Anthropic was blacklisted by the US Department of Defense in February for
refusing to let Claude be used for autonomous weapons and mass
surveillance. The dispute cost Anthropic a $200m contract and is now in the

courts. OpenAI was then criticised from the other side after it struck a deal
to replace Anthropic. Altman later described the move as being “complex
but the right decision with extremely difficult brand consequences and very
negative PR for us in the short term”.
n More recently, the US Department of Commerce issued an export control
directive late on June 12 to suspend all access by foreign nationals to
Anthropic’s Claude Mythos 5 and less powerful Fable 5 models because of

security concerns.
n Although it had to temporarily disable access to all customers to ensure
compliance, its reputation for having the most powerful models will have
benefitted (see our report AI twist in the tale for Anthropic’s Fable and
Mythos). The silver lining may eventually eclipse the cloud amid a branding
boost that money can’t buy. However, alternatives to US models are also
likely to benefit from concerns that access could be switched off without

warning.
Most broadly, the makers of the best-known chatbots may pay a price for being
seen as proxies for all of AI when controversy hits the industry.
n Hot topics include cybersecurity. The leaders of the Five Eyes cybersecurity
agencies of the US, UK, Australia, Canada and New Zealand warned in a
joint statement last week that frontier models are likely to supercharge
cyber threats: “The timeline is not years, it is months”.
The biggest underlying popular concern is what AI will mean for jobs.

n The leaders of both Anthropic and OpenAI have moderated their

### Page 12

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 12 Deutsche Bank AG
predictions of severe disruption in recent weeks, with Amodei pivoting to
say automation would dramatically increase productivity and Altman
saying the impact on jobs has been less than he expected.
n One challenge is that AI has become a scapegoat for job cuts at companies
ranging from Block (4,000 employees, or nearly half the workforce) to
Atlassian (10 percent of roles) and even British American Tobacco (with AI

cited as one of a number of factors in a planned one-fifth reduction in its
workforce).
n However, the tide has begun to turn on predictions of catastrophic job
losses. A slump in entry-level graduate jobs may have been more due to
rising interest rates and remote work than AI – and may be reversing. And
business leaders like Amazon Web Services' CEO Matt Garman have said
eliminating the pipeline of talent makes no sense.
Nowhere has the AI backlash become more focused than over data centres.

n As fast as these “AI factories” are mushrooming around the US and the
world, so are concerns – both justified and unjustified – about them
overwhelming the electricity grid, forcing up power prices, and sucking
rivers dry for water to cool their chips.
n How the leading model makers, hyperscalers and infrastructure
companies handle the issue could determine whether it remains contained
or unleashes moratoriums, regulation or swingeing taxes that put the entire

boom at risk.
n In the US, home to half of the world’s data centres, opposition to “AI
factories” is becoming a rare area of bipartisan consensus, uniting
prominent Democratic Representative Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez and
independent Senator Bernie Sanders on the left, and populist strategist
Steve Bannon on the right.
n Maine recently passed a moratorium on larger data centres while, across
the Atlantic, data-centre hub Ireland has introduced a “Bring Your Own

Power” policy that requires new facilities to generate their own electricity
or secure renewable energy contracts.
Data centers are – literally – close to home for voters.
n That makes them ripe fodder for targeted lobbying and grassroots
campaigns in a way that vague fears about long-term job losses are not. The
US midterm elections in November will be an important litmus test of the
political consequences of local pressure.
n A Gallup poll in May found that 71 percent of Americans would oppose a

data centre being built in their area. At least 75 projects worth a record
$130bn were blocked or delayed in the first quarter, according to Data
Center Watch.
n Tech companies have done their bit to respond. Microsoft, for example,
said in its most recent data centre announcement last week that it would
create thousands of jobs at the facility in Pecos, Texas; pay to ensure it does
not cause a rise in electricity prices; and replenish more water than it uses.

“We are approaching this project as a new neighbour,” it said.
The IPOs of the biggest AI companies could also have repercussions in the
geopolitical contest for technological supremacy, raising their status to that of
strategic national assets.

### Page 13

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 13
n That could encourage other powers to accelerate support for their own
national champions, like China’s “AI tigers” and France’s Mistral, to avoid
becoming dependent on US-controlled foundation models. Other
countries may find themselves having to choose to align with one centre of
power or the other.
4. New era for comparing valuations to peers – and the past
Part of the anxiety about an AI bubble is the lack of pure-play, public, investable

companies to act as a benchmark. A listed OpenAI or Anthropic would fill a huge
gap.
n Sentiment about AI has driven much of the market since the launch of
ChatGPT, with anticipated gains from investment and productivity helping
to explain the two-thirds rise in the S&P 500 Index during that period.
n That rise has been bumpy, soaring and swooning from week to week, as
one area after another, such as software, semiconductors or infrastructure
companies, has come into favour and then fallen out again. Investors have

crowded into trades on a concentrated and shifting list of potential AI
enablers and adopters, and away from prospective casualties.
n To give one example, the Magnificent Seven biggest technology stocks
have whipsawed over the past month amid concerns about how much of
their AI capital expenditure will pay off, shedding as much as 12 percent by
late last week. They have tripled over the past three-and-a-half years to
account for almost a third of the S&P 500.

n That makes investing in AI right now feel a bit like going to a horse race and

having to bet on the jockeys and the condition of the track because the
horses themselves are unavailable.
Of course, there is no guarantee that any filing ends up in a successful IPO.
n The San Francisco-based co-working company WeWork was valued at

### Page 14

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 14 Deutsche Bank AG
$47bn in early 2019 but failed to gain traction when it filed its prospectus in
August that year. It eventually went public in 2021 at a heavily reduced
valuation and ended up filing for bankruptcy in late 2023.
Another way that major IPOs could remove some distortions is by acting as a
release valve for private markets, enabling long-awaited exits from investors.
n Private-equity companies are sitting on a record backlog of unsold

companies, thanks in part to geopolitical disruption and fears of a
“SaaSpocalypse”, according to data from Pitchbook and others. The
median age of still-held assets continued to trend upwards from three years
in 2022 to four years in 2025 as exit activity stalled.
Despite concerns that the boom turns to bust, there are differences in the IPO
markets from the height of the dot-com bubble.
n Overall valuations are clearly elevated by historical standards, with the

Shiller Cyclically Adjusted Price-Earnings (CAPE) ratio for the S&P 500
recently surpassing 40, nearing the record 44 it reached at the peak of the
dot-com bubble.
n However, the IPOs coming to market are more mature and more profitable.
n Back in 1999 to 2000, the median age of venture-capital-backed tech
startups going public shrank to about 4.5 years, according to University of
Florida data. Since then, the median age has been relatively stable at ten-

and-a-half years and was 12 years last year.
n Only 6 percent of those tech startups going public at the height of the dot-
com bubble were profitable, compared with 22 percent since 2010, and
they had a median opening price-to-sales ratio of over 40, four times higher
than the average since 2010.

Perhaps the greatest insurance against a bubble is the fixation of today’s senior
investment managers on the dot-com bust that scarred them in their formative
years in the market.

### Page 15

1 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 15
n For them, mega IPOs bring back memories of the IPO of Netscape in August
1995, widely regarded as the start of the dot-com bubble.However, there is
just as important a lesson in how long it took for that bubble to go bad
despite prophesies of doom and gloom right from day one.
n Bloomberg published an article two days after the IPO on August 9, 1995,
entitled “Why investors say Netscape shows a market poised for a fall” and

warns “Be worried, very worried”. “Netscape was a little insane,” says a
trader quoted in the article, after the stock doubled on its first day of trading,
giving the lossmaking company a $2.9bn market value. “It certainly hints at
more frothiness in the market and that’s usually the sign of the beginning
of the end of a bull market”.
n Yet in the end, the Nasdaq shot up from 1,005 on the day of Netscape’s IPO,
not peaking until almost five years later, at over 5,000 on March 10, 2000.

It was only then that it dramatically fell back, reaching a trough of about
1,100 in October 2002.
n As for Netscape, it was bought by America Online (AOL) for $10bn in 1999,
and then vanished in the fallout from AOL’s record-breaking $124bn
purchase of Time Warner in 2001.
Yet Amazon, another star of the times, shows the power of patience – and picking
a winner.
n Adjusting for stock splits, the shares debuted at 7.5 cents in the company's
$54m IPO on May 15, 1997. After the roller coaster of the dot-com bubble

and crash, the shares took a decade to recapture their early peak – and then
continued to rise, reaching almost $275 a month ago.
n Yet on the day of its IPO, when the stock rose 31 percent, a Pennsylvania-
based fund manager told Bloomberg: “I personally am going to stay away
from it. I don't think it's been proven that one can make a lot of money over
the web.”

Document 170: AI能赚多少钱，取决于能从人类工资池里拿走多少

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI能赚多少钱，取决于能从人类工资池里拿走多少.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-07-03
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

AI 能赚多少钱，不取决于软件市场有多⼤，⽽取决于能从⼈类⼯资池⾥拿⾛多少。国⾦证
券报告测算，美国 1.45 万亿美元薪资已暴露在 AI 冲击下，⽽头部公司 Anthropic 年收⼊仅
470 亿美元，渗透率才 3.2% 。空间巨⼤，但代价是：⾼薪岗位的⼯资正被 AI 重新定价。
摘要：
AI ⼤模型公司到底能赚多少钱？国⾦证券最新报告给出了⼀个颠覆性的答案：别盯着软件市场看
了，要看⼈类的⼯资单。
企业买 AI 不是为了赶时髦，是冲着省钱去的。⽤ AI 替代部分⼈⼒、提升效率、压缩成本，这才是
企业愿意掏钱的真实原因。所以 AI 收⼊的真正天花板，不是软件市场规模，⽽是能被 AI 重新定
价的⼯资池有多⼤。国⾦证券将其称之为， “ 可被 AI 重新定价的⼯资池 ” 。
国⾦证券最新研报算了⼀笔账：美国约 10.83 万亿美元的年⼯资总额中，已有 1.45 万亿美元暴露
在 AI 的冲击范围内 —— 也就是说，这些岗位的⼯作内容， AI 能⼲，或者能帮⼀⼤半。
那 AI 公司从这笔钱⾥赚了多少？拿头部公司 Anthropic 来说，年化收⼊约 470 亿美元，只占了 1.45

万亿的 3.2% 。换个说法，切⾛的连零头都不到。
⼯资池，⽽⾮软件市场，才是 ARR 的估值锚
国⾦证券报告指出，理解本轮 AI 收⼊ " 史诗级增⻓上限 " 最直观的⽅式，是计算 " 可被 AI 重新定价
的⼯资池 " 究竟有多⼤。
报告将不同职业对 AI 技术的暴露度与美国劳⼯统计局（ BLS ） 2025 年职业就业与薪资调查
（ OEWS 2025 ）中 830 个职位进⾏匹配估算。结果显⽰，在美国约 10.83 万亿美元的总薪资收⼊
中，按 Anthropic 的实际观察暴露⼝径，约 1.45 万亿美元⼯资成本已处于 AI 技术暴露范围内，占
⽐ 13.4% ；若采⽤ OpenAI/Eloundou 的理论暴露度⼝径，潜在影响规模则可达约 5.68 万亿美元，
占⽐超过 52% 。
按就业⼈数计算，美国约 1.56 亿就业⼈⼝中，实际暴露⼈数约为 1835 万，占⽐ 11.8% ；理论暴
露⼈数则⾼达约 6830 万，占⽐ 43.9% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

报告强调， 1.45 万亿美元的⼯资成本应被理解为 " 在当前渗透率和技术能⼒下， ARR 收⼊的理想
上限 " ，且这⼀上限还⾯临折价 —— 企业可能仅需 1 万美元 AI ⽀出即可等效替代 10 万美元⼈⼯成
本。即便如此，当前⼤模型商数百亿美元量级的 ARR ，相对于上述薪资池规模，渗透率依然极
低。
AI 冲击呈现 " ⾼薪偏向 " ，知识型岗位⾸当其冲
与过去⾃动化主要冲击制造业和重复性体⼒劳动不同，本轮 AI 更直接触及⾼⼯资、知识密集型和
服务业岗位。
报告数据显⽰，职业对 AI 技术的理论暴露度相对于年度平均薪资分布存在明显右偏 —— ⾼收⼊⼈
群⾯临的 AI 暴露度显著⾼于中低收⼊⼈群。以具体职业为例，收⼊分位数最低的群体（如洗⾐房
员⼯、烘焙师、轮胎⼯） AI 暴露度普遍偏低；⽽⾼收⼊群体中，⾦融产品经理（收⼊分位数
96.6% ，暴露度 78.6% ）、 HR 经理（收⼊分位数 95.3% ，暴露度 76% ）以及航天⼯程师（收⼊分
位数 92.5% ，暴露度 89.3% ）均⾯临较⾼替代⻛险。

从⾏业维度看，理论暴露度最⾼的三个⾏业依次为计算机与数学（ 87.6% ）、商业与⾦融
（ 78.2% ）以及法律（ 78.0% ）。然⽽，实际观察到的暴露度排序与理论值并不⼀致，实际暴露
度最⾼的⾏业为计算机与数学（ 35.3% ）、办公室与⾏政⽀持（ 33.2% ）以及销售相关职位
（ 24.6% ）。
这⼀落差揭⽰出， AI 对劳动⼒的替代并⾮单纯由模型能⼒决定，还受到⼯作属性、责任归属和组
织流程的约束。法律⾏业涉及利益协调、诉讼策略判断及终⾝责任承担；⾦融服务依赖客⼾关
系与⾮标化信息判断；相⽐之下，编程类岗位因⼯作对象明确、反馈链条短，实际替代进展更
快。
计算机⾏业 " ⼀视同仁 " ，⾦融⾏业分化显著
在实际暴露度最⾼的前 20 个职业中，有 8 个归属于计算机与数学⼤类，涉及就业⼈数约 159 万，
占该⾏业总⼈数的 30.2% 。报告指出，对于计算机⾏业⽽⾔，薪资⾼低与 AI 暴露度之间并⽆必然
联系 —— ⾯对 AI 冲击，全⾏业接近 " ⼀视同仁 " ，这凸显出该⾏业在技术迭代下的整体脆弱性。

⾦融⾏业则呈现出截然不同的分化格局。由于部分岗位需要承担责任（如审计、会计），且不同
岗位间⼯作产出的标准化程度参差不⻬，⾦融⾏业整体实际暴露度偏低，但内部分化明显。其

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

中， Market Research Analysts 实际暴露度达 64.8% ， Financial and Investment Analysts 达
57.2% ，⾯临较⼤替代⻛险；⽽其他需要客⼾关系维护和⾮标判断的岗位，暴露度则相对较低。
从薪资暴露总额来看， 1.45 万亿美元的实际暴露薪资基本集中于五⼤⾏业：办公室与⾏政⽀持
（ 2896 亿美元）、商业与⾦融业（ 2474 亿美元）、管理岗位（ 2217 亿美元）、计算机与数学
（ 2152 亿美元）以及销售相关职位（ 1995 亿美元）。报告认为，这为专⽤⼤模型 to B 业务的发展
提供了⽅向参考：追求确定性可深耕⾏政、计算机、⾦融等已出现明显替代的⾏业；追求 "0 到 1

的业务突破 " ，则教育和医疗诊断等⾏业仍具较⼤潜⼒。
替代不等于失业，但薪资重构已在路上
报告明确区分了 " 暴露 " 与 " 替代 " 的概念：暴露意味着任务可能被 AI 辅助、⾃动化或重新组织，但
并不意味着这些⼯资收⼊会等⽐例消失。真正决定 AI 经济影响的，仍是企业采⽤速度、模型能
⼒边界、组织流程改造和监管约束。
然⽽，报告同时指出， AI 的宏观影响不会简单表现为就业数量的线性下降。更可能出现的路径
是：部分单⼀职责岗位被替代，⼤量多职责岗位被重组；部分⼯资成本被压缩，更多劳动过程
被重新定价。尤其是 AI Agent 具有 " ⼯资越⾼、替代率越⾼ " 的属性，这使得 AI 对收⼊消费端的潜
在冲击可能更为深远。
对投资者⽽⾔，报告的核⼼结论是： AI 收⼊端的中期空间，不应只从软件市场规模理解，⽽应从
更⼤的劳动⼒成本池中寻找估算锚定。当前⼤模型商的 ARR 渗透率仍处于极低⽔平，但这枚硬
币的另⼀⾯，是⼈类薪资结构正在⾯临⼀场尚未充分定价的系统性重组。

媒体报道 Anthropic 已开始⾃主研发 AI 芯⽚的早期⼯作，导致美国半导体公司股价集体下
跌。费城半导体指数收跌 5.44% 。闪迪收跌 14% 、美光收跌 5.5% 、英特尔收跌 5.3% 、英伟
达收跌 1.4% 。半导体下跌带动纳斯达克 100 由涨转跌，美股盘初因疲软的⾮农就业数据⼀
度⾛⾼。
摘要：
报道称 Anthropic 已开始⾃主研发 AI 芯⽚的早期⼯作，并与三星就潜在的制造合作进⾏了洽谈，
导致美国半导体公司股价周四盘中下跌，带动纳斯达克 100 由涨转跌，美股盘初因疲软的⾮农就
业数据⼀度⾛⾼。
据 The Information 报道， Anthropic PBC 正与三星电⼦洽谈合作，希望由后者担任其定制⼈⼯智
能（ AI ）芯⽚的制造合作伙伴。 Anthropic 的芯⽚计划仍处于早期阶段，公司尚未确定这款处理

器的具体⽤途、性能⽬标，以及将如何集成到服务器中。
Anthropic 向 The Information 表⽰，亚⻢逊的 Trainium 芯⽚、⾕歌的 TPU （张量处理器）以及英
伟达的 GPU ，未来仍将是公司计算战略的核⼼组成部分。
消息公布后，半导体公司股价集体下跌。费城半导体指数收跌 5.44% 。闪迪收跌 14% 、美光收跌
5.5% 、英特尔收跌 5.3% 、英伟达收跌 1.4% 。半导体下跌带动纳斯达克 100 收跌 1.6% ，美股盘初
因疲软的⾮农就业数据⼀度⾛⾼。
欧洲芯⽚股同样普遍承压： ASML 收跌 3.9% 、 ASM International 收跌 5.6% 、 BE Semiconductor
Industries 收跌 7% 、 Aixtron 收跌 9.9% 、 Nokia 收跌 5.3% 。
随着 AI 服务需求激增，各⼤ AI 公司正寻求实现芯⽚供应多元化，以满⾜不断增⻓的算⼒需求。上
个⽉， OpenAI 发布了与 Broadcom 合作开发的⾸款定制 AI 芯⽚。这家⽣成式 AI 公司希望通过定制

运⾏模型所需的关键硬件，提⾼整体计算效率。

2 of 8 June 29, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Latin America’s Market Resurgence
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Investor attention in international markets has been largely focused on the dominance
of the Asia-Pacific in 2026. Equity returns in Asia have led the global market this year,
and its regional weighting in the MSCI Emerging Markets (EM) Index has risen to
close to 85%— almost five percentage points higher than at the start of the year. But

Asian Equities have not been the only strong performers in global markets over recent
quarters. While it remains a relatively small share of international market capitalization,
Latin America has also staged a recent resurgence, breaking above its 2008 financial
crisis peak for the first time over the past six months. The Latin American market
(measured by MSCI EM Latin America Index) outpaced the rest of the world in 2025 as
International Equities rebounded. And so far this year, the region is ahead of the S&P

500 and all but the highest-flying AI-linked markets in Asia.
Equity returns in Latin America remain highly correlated with global commodity prices,
particularly in metals and energy. And the surge in both precious and industrials metals
prices over the past year has coincided with a period of outperformance for the region—
just as at past peaks in 2008, 2011, 2018 and 2022 (Exhibits 1A and 1B).
Exhibit 1: Latin American Markets Have Peaked With Commodity Prices Over Recent Cycles.

A) Metals Prices versus Latin America Equities. B) Energy Prices Versus Latin America Equities.

In the near term, oil and gas prices may continue to weaken on further easing of supply

constraints in the Middle East. And the potential for the Fed to join the European Central
Bank and the Bank of Japan in raising interest rates could also put cyclical price pressure
on the wider commodity complex. But a number of markets across Latin America should
nonetheless be exposed to longer-term growth expected in demand for key metals and
minerals used in data centers as the AI infrastructure investment boom continues and in
clean energy equipment as countries look to strengthen their energy security in the wake

of the Iran conflict. Mexico and Brazil for example, the largest markets in the region,
are among the leading global producers of silver and graphite, respectively, while Chile
dominates global production and reserves in copper and lithium, accounting for 20%-plus
of the total output for both critical minerals (Exhibit 2).
Furthermore, Brazil in particular is emerging as a more viable global supplier of the 17
rare earth elements (REEs) used across a range of applications including electric vehicles,

wind turbines, robotics, defense hardware, medical equipment and industrial magnets.
Though it is currently still dwarfed by China in REE production, Brazil’s reserves are second
only to China’s, accounting for around 20% of the global total. And it is widely viewed as
a potential future alternative to China in supplying Western markets such as the U.S. and
Europe—both of which are increasing their level of investment into Brazil’s REE industry.
Investment Implications

Latin America has widely gone
unrecognized as one of the leading
global equity markets over recent
quarters. And though nearer-term
challenges from lower energy prices
and potential shifts in external trade
conditions and external monetary policy
could interrupt the recent advance, we
nonetheless see shifts in local policy
direction, Brazil’s potential as a leading
producer of rare earths and exposure to
critical inputs into the growth in clean
energy and AI investment as longer-

term regional supports.

Recent shifts in Latin America’s policy direction have also been welcomed by investors.
Starting with Argentina in 2023, Chile in 2025 and most recently Colombia and Peru earlier
this month, incoming governments have pledged or moved to enact fiscal consolidation,
encourage free trade and deregulation, increase privatizations of state-owned enterprises

and boost economic growth rates—a partial reversal of the so-called Pink Tide that saw less
market-friendly administrations assume leadership in a number of regional economies in
the late 2010s and early 2020s. Argentina for example has been one of the top-performing
markets in the region over recent years, receiving a string of credit rating upgrades from
S&P , Moody’s and Fitch in 2024, 2025 and 2026. A major exception to this trend, however,
has been Latin America’s largest economy, Brazil, where electoral victory for the incumbent

frontrunner in October’s presidential election would likely mean higher public spending, wider
fiscal deficits and a tougher stance on foreign investment into local resource sectors.
External trade and monetary policy are also likely to play an important role in the outlook for
key markets in Latin America. For Mexico, the region’s second-largest economy, a joint review
of the U.S.-Mexico-Canada Agreement set to begin later this week on July 1 could have major

implications for trade and investment in Mexico’s manufacturing sectors. The two main potential
policy risks for Mexico are likely to be 1) higher thresholds for rules of origin that favor U.S.
employment, and 2) more stringent rules on direct investment from China into the Mexican auto
industry to limit its presence in North America for both economic and national security purposes.
Meanwhile a shift toward tighter U.S. monetary policy in the second half of the year could present

another hurdle for the region in the nearer term. Current account balances have improved for most
countries in Latin America over the past two years. But within the MSCI EM Latin America Index all
but Peru remains in deficit, while Brazil and Colombia have both deteriorated to more than 2% of
gross domestic product (GDP)—among the highest within EMs. This potentially leaves the region
more vulnerable to higher U.S. rates than surplus market in Asia. But the general improvement in

balance of payments positions and the likelihood of only modest adjustments to U.S. monetary
policy as opposed to a sustained tightening cycle should limit the impact. Furthermore, against a
backdrop of rising global rates and multidecade lows in S&P 500 dividend yields, Latin American
yields are among the highest globally, standing at close to 6% in Brazil and Colombia.
Latin America has widely gone unrecognized as one of the leading global equity markets over

recent quarters. And though nearer-term challenges from lower energy prices, potential shifts
in external trade conditions and potential changes to external monetary policy could interrupt
the recent advance, we nonetheless see shifts in local policy direction, and Brazil’s potential
as a leading producer of rare earths and exposure to critical inputs into the growth in clean
energy and AI investment as longer-term regional supports.

4 of 8 June 29, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Second-Half Scenarios: Revisiting the Base, Bull and Bear Cases
Marci McGregor, Managing Director and Head of CIO Portfolio Strategy
The first half of the year is quickly fading into the rearview mirror. As the next six
months come into focus, we refresh our base, bull and bear scenarios for the remainder
of 2026. The verdict: The base case holds steady, the bull case is picking up momentum,

and the bear case looks less likely than it did earlier this year.
Base case: Unchanged at 55%. The base case remains the anchor (Exhibit 3). Despite
a steady drumbeat of headline risk, fundamentals remain sound, and the strong growth
outlook is intact. The labor market remains far from recessionary territory, with hiring
surging in May, and the unemployment rate holding steady at 4.3%. Consumers are
still in good shape with Bank of America Institute data showing credit and debit card

spending rising 5.1% year-over-year (YoY) in May, the fastest pace in nearly four years.
The recent oil and gas spikes seem unlikely to translate into an economywide resurgence
in inflation, although sticky price pressures and healthy job growth could still leave room
for limited Fed hikes later in the year.
For markets, the picture is similarly constructive. S&P 500 earnings-per-share are
expected to grow at a double-digit YoY pace, while corporate margins remain resilient.

A multiyear technology-driven capital expenditures (capex) cycle is well underway, and
continued AI adoption should provide a meaningful productivity tailwind over time.
Against this backdrop, Equity market participation could continue to broaden, with EMs
and Small- and Mid-caps improving further. In our view, this remains the most likely path
for the remainder of 2026, with a 55% possibility outcome.
Bull case: Increased to 25%. Upside risks to our base case have emerged with the

bull scenario gathering momentum. We raise the possibility outcome to 25% from 15%,
with stronger earnings growth doing much of the heavy lifting. Estimates for both 2026
and 2027 have moved meaningfully higher in recent weeks, with revisions outpacing
historical averages. Easing geopolitical tensions have also meaningfully improved the
outlook. Stronger profits, improving productivity and broader participation below the
index level could help extend the cycle in the bull case even with valuations elevated.

In this upside scenario, inflation would continue to cool as oil and gas prices ease,
allowing the Fed to remain on an extended pause or deliver, at most, one potential hike. A
steeper yield curve could emerge alongside stronger growth expectations and a resilient
macro backdrop. The AI capex cycle could also keep accelerating, lifting Technology
and supporting investment across data centers, power generation, grid modernization,
industrial automation, aerospace and defense. Putting it all together, the combined bull

case and base case scenario now accounts for 80% of our 2026 market outlook.
Bear case: Decreased to 15%. The bear case is still worth monitoring, but it looks less
concerning than it did earlier in the year. We have lowered its possibility outcome to 15%
from 25%, as several of the most disruptive risks to this market have eased, at least for
now. Diplomatic progress toward ending the U.S/Iran war has reduced the probability of
tensions boiling over into a broader conflict. The resulting decline in oil and gas prices

has helped relieve one of the most immediate pressure points for inflation and consumer
confidence. Other potential shocks have also failed to materialize: The largest initial
public offering in history was absorbed with ease, and the Fed’s leadership transition has
been relatively smooth, even if the new chair’s hawkish tone has raised some eyebrows.
For the bear case to reassert itself, we would likely need to see a renewed flare-up in
geopolitical tensions, a reversal higher in oil, and inflation moving well above target. A

key risk would be a sharp rise in rates driven by a more aggressive policy response, which
could pressure Equities lower and put both earnings and valuations at risk. The growth
Portfolio Considerations

for upside to our base-case market

concerns. This confluence of factors could bring technology-bubble concerns back into 
focus and shift market leadership toward defensives.
Grizzly case: Still unlikely at 5%. The grizzly bear scenario remains the deep- tail 
risk, and would require a convergence of negative earnings and geopolitical and 
macroeconomic outcomes that could push the S&P 500 into bear-market territory. In our 
view, it remains the least likely path.

6 of 8 June 29, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
From Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Investment Strategist
The market narrative has flipped from how soon the Fed might cut to whether hikes are back
on the table. Prior to the U.S./Iran conflict, markets had fully priced in two Fed rate cuts by
year-end. Today, markets are pricing at least one Fed rate hike. BofA Global Research now

anticipates three 25 basis-point (bps) hikes in September, October and December, versus prior
expectations for a prolonged Fed pause and eventual cuts in 2027. What matters now is how
this shift in expectations evolves as new data comes in.
This repricing reflects a combination of renewed inflation pressures and more hawkish Fed
signaling. Headline personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation rose to a three-year
high of 4.1% YoY in May, up from 2.9% pre-conflict, while core PCE, which excludes food

and energy, climbed to 3.4% from 3% a few months ago—roughly 60 bps above the level a
year ago. At the same time, the Fed’s June Federal Open Market Committee (FOMC) meeting
pointed to a firmer policy path. While rates were held steady, the Fed’s median projection for
the policy rate at year-end rose to 3.8% from 3.4%, with nine of 18 policymakers expecting at
least one hike this year and six expecting more than one.
That said, the shift in expectations is not without nuance. The Fed continues to emphasize a

data-dependent approach, particularly under new Fed Chair Kevin Warsh, who has historically
expressed caution about forward guidance. The fact that these hawkish projections based
on geopolitical disruptions were released essentially at the same time a U.S./Iran ceasefire
agreement led to the reopening of the Strait of Hormuz underscores just how quickly
conditions can change. More broadly, history suggests the Fed’s policy projections have not
always aligned with its realized policy path (Exhibit 4A).

For investors, the key question is what a potential return to rate hikes means for Equities.
Historical outcomes have varied depending on the broader macro backdrop (Exhibit 4B).
Across the last six hiking cycles, equity returns were volatile in the early stages but generally
improved over time, with annualized returns positive over the full cycle in every episode.
For now, the latest forecasts suggest any renewed Fed tightening would be modest and

relatively short-lived. While inflation risks have reemerged, it remains uncertain whether they
will translate into a sustained reacceleration, particularly if geopolitical pressures continue
to ease. We view the recent hawkish repricing as a risk to monitor rather than a structural
shift to the outlook, with the more significant risk being a materially more aggressive Fed
tightening response if inflation remains persistently elevated.
Importantly, the economy has proven resilient in a higher-rate environment in recent years,

suggesting it may be able to absorb some modest Fed tightening. We therefore remain
constructive on U.S. Equities, supported by strong earnings momentum and long-term
thematic tailwinds.

in interest rates driven by a more

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Forward Total Return Index is an index that tracks the performance of the S&P 500 while assuming all cash dividends are automatically reinvested. While the standard S&P 500
only measures the raw price appreciation of its stocks, the TR version reflects the true compounding growth an investor actually experiences.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index is an index that tracks the performance of the S&P 500 while assuming all cash dividends are automatically reinvested. While the standard S&P 500 only

measures the raw price appreciation of its stocks, the TR version reflects the true compounding growth an investor actually experiences.
London Metal Exchange Metal Index (LMEX) is a basket benchmark that tracks the global performance of six primary industrial metals. The index represents aluminum (42.8%), copper
(31.2%), zinc (14.8%), lead (8.2%), nickel (2%), and tin (1%).
MSCI Emerging Markets Latin America Index captures large and mid cap representation across 5 Emerging Markets (EM) countries in Latin America.

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and

interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia
First impressions – May CPI
By Neha Sharma&Sian Fenner
12:38 June 24 2026 Share
The May CPI fell –0.7%mth to be up 4.0%yr. Trimmed mean rose 0.4%mth/3.6%yr.
The May CPI fell –0.7%mth to be up 4.0%yr. This was below our estimate of 4.4%yr and market
expectations of 4.3%yr. May is typically a seasonally softer month for the CPI. In seasonally adjusted
terms, the CPI dipped –0.1%mth, compared with our estimate of +0.2%mth. The key drivers behind the

softer May outcome were transport, clothing & footwear and recreation & culture. The latter two
accounted for nearly all of the miss in our monthly result. Balancing this was a firmer result in the

### Page 2

housing component, with a broad-based pick-up across electricity, rents and new dwelling costs.
Despite the weakness in the headline, underlying pressures are picking up and suggest second-round
effects from the Middle East conflict are broadening. Trimmed mean inflation printed at 0.4%mth and
3.6%yr, this was in line with our expectations but higher than the market’s 3.5%yr. The six-month
annualised pace lifted from 3.2% to 3.5%.
In terms of the key surprises to our forecast:

Downside surprises
Upside surprises
The May data provide a stronger signal that second-order effects from the Middle East supply shock
are becoming more visible across consumer prices. Oil prices have come off their highs and policy is
still dampening parts of fuel inflation, for now, but higher fuel, transport and commodity costs are
continuing to feed through to a broader range of categories. This is consistent with firms passing on at
least part of the increase in input costs, including energy, freight, plastics and chemicals.

The key question is whether these price increases reverse as cost pressures ease, or whether they
persist. Postal costs fell in May, as the fuel surcharge was reduced, showing some pass-through can
unwind. But the risk is that pass-through in other areas is stickier, particularly across services. The
increase in hairdressing prices, for example, could reflect firms adjusting prices not just for higher fuel
and transport costs, but for a broader expectation of higher operating costs. That makes the May

outcome less about whether pass-through is occurring, and more about how broad and persistent it
becomes.
Today’s trimmed mean outcome came in line with expectations and is on track to rise 1.0% in Q2,
taking annual trimmed mean inflation to 3.8%yr. As such, we retain our view that further cash rate
increases are coming, with the next hike likely at the August meeting.
The May data reinforce the RBA’s concern that inflation remains too high and that a period of slower

growth will be needed to return inflation to target. Even with oil prices off their highs, and other
commodity prices easing to a lesser extent, we expect some further pass-through from still elevated
Recreation & culture was the key miss, owing to bigger-than expected falls in domestic (–12.1%mth
vs –5.5%mth) and international travel (–0.8%mth vs 3.8%mth).
Clothing & footwear fell by more than expected (–2.9%mth vs –1.8%mth).
Some of the other areas of weakness were in household textiles (usually weak in May), vegetables

and medical appliances.
Housing rose 0.5%mth vs 0.3%mth expected. New dwelling costs was the main surprise here, lifting
0.9%mth/5.6%yr, this was the strongest monthly increase since December 2022. We had flagged
upside risks here given evidence from price notifications data. Rents rose 0.4%mth against
expectations of 0.3%mth.
Automotive fuel fell by less than expected (–11.9%mth vs –14.5%mth).

### Page 3

Related articles
fuel and commodity costs over coming months, particularly as policy supports unwind. Wage costs
could add further pressure in the second half, especially across market services.
Housing inflation will also remain a key pressure point. It accounts for around 20% of the CPI basket
and is typically a source of persistence in trimmed mean inflation. That keeps upside risks front of mind
for the RBA and leaves further tightening firmly on the table.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
MAY CPI
PREVIEW
Underlying pressures persist
19 June 2026
Key points
• We expect May CPI to fall –0.3%mth, 
which will still see the annual pace lift to 
4.4%yr. On a seasonally adjusted basis, 
we pencil in a modest 0.2%mth rise.
• Transport is expected to again be 
the main drag due to lower fuel 
prices, with clothing & footwear also 
subtracting. This will be partly offset 
by modest gains in food and housing.
• The monthly trimmed mean is forecast 
at 0.4%mth, lifting the annual pace 
to 3.6%yr. Market services excluding 
volatiles is expected to be flat in the 
month; excluding both volatiles & holiday 
travel is expected to rise 0.2%mth.
• Downside risks are centred on timing, 
with uncertainty over the pace and 
extent of price adjustments. Some 
measures suggest pressures moderated 
in May but remain elevated. The key 
upside risk is a faster pass-through 
of higher construction costs.
